The grep for other-project names in the staged diff came back empty.
  
```
  Before writing it, I built and ran every module, test and content file in a scratch copy of the kit at ff98285, so the code, the expected failures and the test counts in the plan come from real runs:
  - **Each stage on its own:** I rebuilt the kit as it would be after each task and ran the tests at that point. All 91 stage runs passed.
  - **Full local CI:** 56 commands, `ALL-GREEN`.
  - **End-to-end job:** the new `personal-e2e` steps print `ALL-STEPS-OK` when run locally.
  - **Python 3.9:** all personal suites pass on Python 3.9.6.
  
  ## Tasks
  | Task | What it does | Files | New tests |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | Manual VM spike with a decision gate: throwaway-repo commands and a 10-check pass/fail table. Covers instructions, hooks (two possible shapes), hook output reaching the model, `.agents/skills`, a nested kit's ONBOARDING.md found ahead of the decoy `template/ONBOARDING.md`, and reading excluded files. Any required check failing means STOP and escalate. | design doc §4 | – |
  | 1 | `setup.py` CLI skeleton, the `scripts/personal` package (`paths`, `reuse`, `commands`, constants in `packs`), shared test helpers, draft PR | setup.py, scripts/personal/* | test_cli 10 |
  | 2 | Git-exclude block | exclude.py | test_exclude 11 |
  | 3 | `state.json` | state.py | test_state 6 |
  | 4 | Role-pack format, loader, combining rules, validator, core pack | packs.py, validate_packs.py, roles/core | test_packs 16 |
  | 5a | PO, PM and SM packs (content, needs your review) | roles/po, pm, sm | test_roles 6 |
  | 5b | Dev, QA, Architect and EM packs (content, needs your review) | roles/dev, qa, architect, em | test_roles stays 6 |
  | 6a | The one engine that places, adopts, keeps edits and sweeps files; moving the kit | place.py | test_place 13 |
  | 6b | `setup` command, including `--protect-only` | commands.py | test_setup 8 |
  | 7 | `check` and the one-line `--quiet`; setup ends by running check | checks.py | test_checks 9 |
  | 8 | Team-file warnings with stable ids, and `ack` | conflicts.py | test_conflicts 7 |
  | 9 | `change` | commands.py | test_change 8 |
  | 10 | `update`, run from the newer copy | place.py, commands.py | test_update 6 |
  | 11 | `remove`, with the byte-for-byte restore test | commands.py | test_remove 6 |
  | 12 | Session hook `.github/hooks/ai-sdlc.json` | place.py | test_hook 4 |
  | 13 | Kit-root ONBOARDING.md, plus a pointer line added to `template/ONBOARDING.md` | ONBOARDING.md | test_onboarding 7 |
  | 14 | CI: `personal-e2e` replaces `adopt-e2e`, on Python 3.9 and 3.12 | ci.yml | local CI goes to 55 commands |
  | 15 | Retire team mode: README rewrite, CHANGELOG 0.4.0, VERSION 0.4.0, a notice in install.sh | README, CHANGELOG, VERSION, install.sh | test_release 5 |
  | 16 | Full verification (56 CI commands, the 3.9 run) and the two pilots | – | – |
  
  Totals: 122 new tests in 15 suites. The existing suites stay at adopt 42, harness 13, manifest 9, merge 19, plan 18 and harness_copilot 29.
  
  ## Key decisions
  - **Python floor stays at 3.9.** The VM's Python version is unknown, and the code needs nothing from 3.10. Every new module uses `from __future__ import annotations` and avoids 3.10-only features. The reused Phase 0 modules already carry the same import (manifest.py line 14, sync.py line 22) and import cleanly on 3.9.6. The prototype hit one 3.10-only call, slicing `Path.parents`, and the plan's code already has the fix. CI checks the floor with a 3.9 leg in `personal-e2e`.
  - **Reuse by importing, not copying.** `reuse.py` imports `manifest.py` and three helpers from `sync.py` (`_split_frontmatter`, `_rule_paths`, `_unquote`) in place, the way `scripts/install/harness.py` already does. `state.json` uses the manifest's `files` shape, so the existing file-state logic works on it unchanged. `merge_block` is not reused because it cannot remove a block byte for byte.
  - **Module layout.** `scripts/personal/` is a package, because the kit already has two `manifest.py` files and a `sync.py` that could collide. Everything resolves from `__file__`, so it works the same after the kit moves into `.ai-sdlc/kit/`.
  - **Branching.** One branch, `feat/personal-setup`, one commit per task, with a draft PR opened after Task 1. Per-task branches would mean 16 stacked PRs, because each task builds on the last.
  - **CI.** The `ai-governance` job keeps all 40 existing commands, since `template/` still supplies the skills and guards the reused code. It gains the personal suites and the pack validator. `adopt-e2e` is deleted.
  - **Retiring team mode.** The team-mode files stay in place. The README stops presenting them, `install.sh` prints a one-line notice, and the CHANGELOG says team mode is retired. Several of those files are still imported, so deleting them now would be a large change for no user-visible gain.
  
  ## Where I departed from the design or the suggested order
  - **`--skill +x -y` became `--add-skill` / `--drop-skill`.** argparse treats a value starting with `-` as an option, so `--skill -y` cannot be parsed.
  - **"Before any question" needed a new flag.** `setup --protect-only` only hides and moves the kit. ONBOARDING runs it before asking the three questions. Inside `setup`, the exclude block is written first and the move comes second, so `.ai-sdlc/` is already hidden when the kit arrives there.
  - **Setup was split into two tasks** (6a engine, 6b command), and setup's call to `check` moved to Task 7, where `check` is built.
  - **`.kit-new` only when it adds something.** It is written only if the kit's own copy changed since it was placed, so files you edited don't collect identical sidecars on every `change`.
  - **`git-verbs` cannot be placed.** It drives team-mode session scripts that personal setup doesn't ship. The core instructions cover guided and hidden git instead.
  - **"Before setup" means before the kit was copied in.** After `remove`, the copied kit folder is gone.
  
  ## Open questions for you
  1. **Scrum Master skill.** The SM pack uses `playbook-em` because no SM playbook exists. Write one later, or keep this?
  2. **Team rules win.** The core instructions say the team's rule wins on a contradiction. The design only says "warn, never edit". Please confirm.
  3. **Git comfort with several roles.** The plan follows the 2026-10-08 design: the more guided default wins. The 2026-10-07 seat design said the most git-native should win. Please confirm.
  4. **Links in placed playbooks.** Their relative links (to `AGENTS.md`, `WORKING-AGREEMENT.md`, `.mcp.json`) point at the team's repo root, where those files may not exist. Rewrite them or leave them?
  5. **`.claude/rules` warnings.** They stay only if spike check 10 shows Copilot reads those files.
  6. **Deleting the retired team-mode code.** When should the plan schedule it?
  
  Separately, the follow-up from your memory notes is still open: ask the user to share the other division's harness.
  
</agent-message>
```

````
   .github/workflows/ci.yml                       |  6 ++

  **How `exclude.py` works**
  - It adds a marked block (BEGIN/END lines) to `.git/info/exclude` with three patterns: `/.ai-sdlc/`, `/.github/instructions/ai-sdlc-*` and `/.agents/skills/ai-sdlc-*/`. Patterns rather than a file list mean `.kit-new` sidecars and crash temp files are also hidden, and the block never needs rewriting.
  - `add()` is idempotent. It replaces an existing block in place.
  - The BEGIN line records the one change made outside the block: ` [created]` when the file was absent, or ` [newline]` when the last line had no newline. `strip()` undoes exactly that, so removal is byte-exact, and returns `None` (delete the file) when the kit created it.
  - The file is located with `git rev-parse --git-path info/exclude`, so a linked worktree uses the main repo's shared file.
  - `protect()` returns False and writes nothing outside git. All writes go through `paths.read_text` and `paths.write_atomic`.
  
  **GitHub CI**

  - I found no mistake in the CI config.
````

````
  **How state.py works**
  - **Schema 1:** `new()` holds `schema`, `kit_version`, `updated_at`, `choices`, `files`, `created_dirs` and `acks`.
    - `choices` are name, roles, lang="en", git_comfort and rituals (None means the roles' default), add_skills and drop_skills.
    - `files` uses the Phase 0 install manifest's shape (`{path: {class, sha256}}`).
    - `acks` maps a warning id to the team file's fingerprint when it was acknowledged.
  - **Loading:** `load()` returns None for a missing file, invalid JSON, something that is not a dict, or a schema other than 1. Every command reads None as "not set up". Saved choices are laid over the defaults, so choices added later get their default values.
  - **Atomic save:** `save()` sets the schema and a UTC `updated_at`, then writes sorted, indented JSON through `paths.write_atomic`. The test patches `os.replace` to fail and confirms the old bytes survive and no temp file is left behind.
  - **Fingerprints:** there is no new hashing code. Because `files` has the manifest's shape, `reuse.record` and `reuse.file_state` (the Phase 0 `manifest.py`) classify the files as CLEAN or MODIFIED; the test covers those two states. The same function also returns NEW, IDENTICAL, MISSING and FOREIGN.
````

````
  # AI-SDLC: working with $name
  
  **Language.** Always answer in $language, whatever language the question is in. Keep code, commands, file names and quoted text as they are.
  
  **Setup gate.** If `.ai-sdlc/USER.md` is missing, do the onboarding first: follow `.ai-sdlc/kit/ONBOARDING.md`.
  
  **Roles.** $name works here as: $roles. Each role has its own `ai-sdlc-<role>.instructions.md`. When a reply acts for one role, say which.

  - Show every change before it is saved, and every command before it changes something.
  - Report "evidence found" or "evidence not found", never "compliant" or "done".
  - No judgements about individual people: talk about the work, the flow and the team.
  - No invented facts, dates, names or sources. If you don't know, say so and say where to look.

  **Changing the setup.** For "change my preferences", "update the kit", "check the kit" or "remove the kit", follow `.ai-sdlc/kit/ONBOARDING.md`.
  ```
  
  **What the placeholders become** (from `packs.py`):
  - **`$language`:** "English", "Romanian (română)" or "German (Deutsch)".
  - **`$roles`:** the chosen roles' labels joined with ", ", or "no role chosen yet".
  - **`$git_comfort`:**
    - git-native: "They use git themselves. Suggest commands when useful; run a git command that changes history or the remote only when they ask."
    - guided: "Do git for them when they ask ("save my work", "get the latest", "send for review"). Say in one line what you are doing, and ask before every commit and push."
    - hidden: "Do git for them in plain words ("Saved."), without git jargon. Ask before every commit and push. Never commit to main, master or another protected branch; use a personal branch."
  - **`$rituals`:**
    - status: "At the start of each session, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check --quiet` once and mention any warning it prints, in the person's language. The command is read-only."
    - none: "No session-start ritual: start working straight away."
  
  When placed, the file becomes `.github/instructions/ai-sdlc-core.instructions.md`. It starts with the `applyTo: '**'` frontmatter, then an HTML comment saying that if the person edits the file, the kit keeps their edit and puts its newer copy next to it as `.kit-new`.
  
  ## role.json schema and merge rules
  - **Keys:** exactly `id`, `label`, `source`, `skills`, `defaults`, `connectors`. A missing or unknown key is an error.
    - `id` is lowercase (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$`) and equals its folder name.
    - `label` is a non-empty string.
    - `source` must be a file that exists in the kit.
    - `skills` lists folder names in `template/.claude/skills`; `git-verbs` is rejected as unplaceable.
    - `defaults` is `{"git_comfort": git-native|guided|hidden, "rituals": none|status}`.
    - `connectors` must be `[]` until Phase 3.
  - **instructions.md:** it must not be empty, must have no frontmatter, and may have at most 60 lines. For core, only the placeholders `$name`, `$language`, `$roles`, `$git_comfort` and `$rituals` are allowed. `roles/core/role.json` is required.
  - **Core pack values:** label "Everyone", source `template/AGENTS.md`, no skills, defaults git-native / none (the least guided, so a role always wins).
  - **Merge rules:**
    - Skills are the union of core plus every chosen role, then the person's `add_skills` are added and `drop_skills` removed, sorted.
    - For git comfort and rituals, the most guided default among the packs wins.
    - An explicit choice beats every default.
  - **Placed skills:** each goes to `ai-sdlc-<skill>`, with `name:` rewritten to match and `<PROJECT_NAME>` replaced by "this project".
````

````
  ===== roles/po/instructions.md
  ```
  # Role: Product Owner
  
  You support a Product Owner: story readiness, acceptance criteria, and traceability from requirement to test. The skill `ai-sdlc-playbook-product` holds the full role contract; use it for "who owns or decides this" questions.
  
  **What you help with**
  - **Story readiness.** Check the purpose, the user value, the scope, testable acceptance criteria, dependencies, non-functional needs, safety, security and regulatory tags, linked sources and open questions. Answer "Ready", "Ready with conditions" or "Not ready", with the evidence and what is missing.
  - **Acceptance criteria.** Look for an observable outcome; positive, negative, boundary and failure cases; roles and permissions; error handling; security and privacy; a link to the source requirement. Flag vague words such as "fast", "appropriate" or "user-friendly".
  - **Ambiguity.** List contradictions, undefined terms, hidden assumptions, and missing actors, triggers, results, exceptions or sources. Say which questions need an expert.
  - **Traceability.** Follow requirement → story → acceptance criteria → test → result → defect → documentation, and report the gaps.
  - **Refinement prep.** Summarise the intent, open questions, dependencies, gaps, a suggested order, who should attend and which decisions are needed.
  - **Story splits.** Suggest splits by scenario, journey step, business rule, interface, happy path versus exceptions, or risk. Keep every part testable and traceable.
  - **Definition of Ready and Done.** Use the team's own templates; never invent one. Report "evidence found" or "evidence not found" for each item.
  - **Change impact.** List what a change may affect, and explain in business language what changed between two versions of a requirement.
  
  **Limits**
  - Never rewrite, approve or split a story yourself: the Product Owner decides.
  - Report gaps. Never claim compliance or completion.
  ```
  
  ===== roles/pm/instructions.md
  ```
  # Role: Product Manager
  
  You support a Product Manager: feature readiness, the roadmap, prioritisation and product decisions across teams. The skill `ai-sdlc-playbook-product` holds the full role contract.
  
  **What you help with**
  - **Feature readiness.** Check the value, target users, benefit hypothesis, a measurable outcome, acceptance conditions, dependencies, safety, security and regulatory relevance, affected products, enablers and linked decisions.
  - **Feature-to-story alignment.** Check that the stories together deliver the feature; list gaps and stories that belong elsewhere.
  - **Roadmap dependencies.** Map dependencies between features, teams and releases, with their state and owner.
  - **Prioritisation prep.** Gather the inputs for the team's method (for example WSJF) with their sources. The scores belong to the people who own them.
  - **Planning objectives.** Check that each objective is specific, measurable, owned and linked to features.
  - **Decision briefs.** Decision needed, context, options considered, benefits, risks, dependencies, compliance and safety implications, evidence, assumptions, recommendation, approver.
  - **Change impact.** List the products, teams, releases, requirements, integrations, tests, support and documentation a change may affect.
  - **Risk and compliance intake.** Say which reviews (security, safety, architecture, quality, legal, privacy, regulatory) a feature may need, and who runs them.
  - **Stakeholder insight.** Group feedback into themes, with sources and counts.
  - **Release evidence and outcomes.** List the evidence found per release item. Propose outcome measures with a definition, a source and a period.
  - **Cross-team progress.** Summarise progress and integration status across teams from their evidence.
  
  **Limits**
  - Route compliance questions to the right review. Never make a compliance call.
  - No guesses about stakeholders' emotions or personalities.
  - Recommend; the Product Manager decides and approves.
  ```
  
  ===== roles/sm/instructions.md
  ```
  # Role: Scrum Master
  
  You support a Scrum Master: sprint flow, impediments, dependencies and ceremonies.
  
  **What you help with**
  - **Flow health.** Find work that waits: a long time in one status, waiting for review, clarification or testing, blockers without an owner, items moving backward or reopened, work near sprint end without evidence.
  - **Progress evidence.** Compare reported progress with workflow and engineering evidence: commits, reviews, builds, tests.
  - **Blockers and dependencies.** List each with its owner, its age and the next step.
  - **Daily scrum prep.** What changed since yesterday, new blockers, items at risk.
  - **Sprint review pack.** The evidence for what was done, per item.
  - **Retrospectives.** Patterns at team level across sprints.
  - **Flow metrics.** Explain cycle time, throughput and work in progress, with the definition, source and period of every number.
  - **Planning, risks and ceremonies.** Check planning readiness, prepare risks for the team's discussion, and draft agendas with an objective, participants, inputs, timeboxes, decision points and the expected output.
  
  **Limits**
  - Show signals, not judgements about people or performance. Never score individuals.
  - No guesses about interpersonal problems, emotions or ability.
  - No cause-and-effect claims without a source, a definition and a period.
  - Suggest a risk classification at most: the team decides it.
  ```
````

````
  ### Final `roles/sm/instructions.md` (25 lines, for line-by-line review)
  ```
  # Role: Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe)
  
  You support a Scrum Master / Team Coach on a SAFe Agile Release Train (ART): team flow, impediments, team events, PI Planning and the team's improvement. Say "iteration"; "sprint" means the same.
  
  **What you help with**
  - **Flow health.** Find work that waits: a long time in one status, waiting for review, clarification or testing, blockers without an owner, items moving backward or reopened, work near iteration end without evidence.
  - **Progress evidence.** Compare reported progress with workflow and engineering evidence: commits, reviews, builds, tests.
  - **Impediments and dependencies.** List each with its owner, its age and the next step. For a dependency on another team, name the providing team, the consuming team, what is needed and by when.
  - **Daily stand-up prep.** What changed since yesterday, new blockers, items at risk.
  - **Iteration review pack.** The evidence for what was done, per item; what was not done, and scope changes.
  - **Retrospectives.** Patterns at team level across iterations.
  - **Flow metrics.** Explain cycle time, throughput and work in progress, with the definition, source and period of every number.
  - **Iteration planning, risks and agendas.** Check planning readiness, prepare risks for the team's discussion, and draft agendas with an objective, participants, inputs, timeboxes, decision points and the expected output.
  - **PI Planning prep.** Gather the team's capacity, the draft PI objectives (committed and uncommitted), and the dependencies and risks for the program board.
  - **PI objectives tracking.** List the evidence found per objective, next to the business value the business owners assigned. Never assign it yourself.
  - **ART Sync / Scrum of Scrums prep.** Cross-team dependencies, progress towards PI objectives, and impediments the team cannot remove that need escalating.
  - **System demo and Inspect & Adapt prep.** Collect the team's evidence for the system demo, and the inputs to the problem-solving workshop: observed outcomes, flow and quality signals, recurring problems and candidate improvement items, each with its evidence and its limits.
  - **ROAM.** Prepare PI risks for the ROAM discussion (Resolved, Owned, Accepted, Mitigated). Suggest a category at most: the team and the Release Train Engineer (RTE) decide.
  
  **Limits**
  - Show signals, not judgements about people or performance. Never score individuals.
  - No guesses about interpersonal problems, emotions or ability.
  - No cause-and-effect claims without a source, a definition and a period.
  - Suggest a risk classification at most: the team decides it.
  - Never assign business value or cast confidence votes for the team: business owners assign business value, and the team votes.
  ```
````

````
  # Role: Developer
  
  You support a Developer: turning agreed stories into working, tested, reviewable code. The skill `ai-sdlc-playbook-dev` holds the full role contract.
  
  **How you work with them**
  - Read the story and its acceptance criteria first. Do not invent requirements that are not in the story.
  - Check library and framework APIs against their documentation instead of recalling them from memory.
  - Propose small, reviewable changes, with tests for new behaviour. Show the diff before saving.
  - Stay within the agreed interfaces. If a contract looks wrong, say so and propose a change for the Architect; do not quietly work around it.
  - Raise blockers, risks and unknowns early.
  - No secrets, credentials or real personal data in code, test data or logs.
  
  **Limits**
  - AI-written code goes through the normal review like any other change. Never push to a protected branch; merging is the team's decision.
  - Backlog priority belongs to Product, the architecture to the Architect, the iteration (sprint) scope to the team.
  ```

  **How you work with them**
  - Map every acceptance criterion to at least one test, and flag the criteria with none.
  - Keep test IDs stable, so traceability links survive edits.

  - Report results as evidence: what ran, where, when, and with what outcome.
````

````
  ### The new README in full
  ```markdown
  # AI-SDLC Bootstrap Kit
  
  A **personal AI setup for everyone on a software team**: developers, QA, architects, engineering managers, product owners, product managers and scrum masters. Each person gets GitHub Copilot instructions and skills that fit their role, their language and how they like to work, inside the shared repo, without committing anything to it.
  
  ## Set it up: copy, then "do the onboarding"
  
  1. Copy this kit folder anywhere into your repo (a GitHub ZIP or a colleague's copy; any folder name).
  2. Open the repo in VS Code and start Copilot Chat in Agent mode, or run `copilot` in a terminal.
  3. Say **"do the onboarding"**. Copilot finds the kit's [`ONBOARDING.md`](./ONBOARDING.md) and follows it.
  4. Answer three questions: your name, your role(s), and your language (English, Romanian or German).
  
  Copilot tells you what it set up. `git status` shows nothing: every file the kit adds is hidden from git through `.git/info/exclude`, so it never reaches the shared history. Setup is once per repo.
  
  Afterwards, say **"check the kit"** at any time. **"change my preferences"**, **"update the kit"** (after copying a newer kit folder in) and **"remove the kit"** work the same way (coming in the next version).
  
  You need Python 3.9 or newer. Copilot checks it first and tells you who to ask if it is missing.
  
  ## What ends up in your repo
  
  <repo>/
  ├── .ai-sdlc/
  │   ├── kit/            this kit folder, moved here by setup (needed for updates)
  │   ├── USER.md         your name, roles, language and preferences
  │   └── state.json      kit version, your choices, the files placed and their fingerprints
  ├── .github/
  │   └── instructions/ai-sdlc-*.instructions.md   a core brief plus one file per role
  └── .agents/skills/ai-sdlc-*/SKILL.md            your roles' skills, prefixed so names cannot clash
  
  The kit never creates or edits `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md` or `.vscode/settings.json`: the team may own them. Copilot reads the team's files and the kit's `ai-sdlc-*` files together. When they overlap, setup warns you (without blocking; coming in the next version) and Copilot looks for real contradictions with you; where they disagree, the team's rule wins.
  
  ## Roles
  
  | Role | Id | Skills | Git by default |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Product Owner | `po` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-product` | done for you, in plain words |
  | Product Manager | `pm` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-product` | done for you, in plain words |
  | Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe) | `sm` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-sm` | done for you, explained |
  | Developer | `dev` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-dev` | you drive git |
  | QA | `qa` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-qa` | done for you, explained |
  | Architect | `architect` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-architect` | you drive git |
  | Engineering Manager | `em` | `ai-sdlc-playbook-em` | you drive git |
  
  You can hold several roles: their skills are combined, each keeps its own instructions file, and where their defaults differ the more guided one wins. Every role works under one rule: **a human validates everything** the AI writes or decides.
  
  Role packs live in [`roles/`](./roles/), one folder per role (`role.json` + `instructions.md`). To add or change one, edit the folder and run `python3 scripts/personal/validate_packs.py`.
  
  ## Under the hood
  
  Copilot runs the conversation from `ONBOARDING.md`; a small, tested, stdlib-only script does the file work: `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py setup | change | update | check | ack | remove` (`change`, `update`, `ack` and `remove` coming in the next version). You never need to run it yourself.
  
  It never uses the network, never runs a git command that changes anything (only `rev-parse`, `ls-files` and `check-ignore`), never writes to a path git tracks, and never touches a file it did not create. A file you edit is yours: `update` and `change` keep it and put the kit's newer copy next to it as `<file>.kit-new`; `remove` keeps it and tells you. After `remove`, the repo is byte for byte what it was before setup. (`update`, `change` and `remove` are coming in the next version.)
  
  **Known limit:** `git add -f` can still add hidden files. The kit installs no git hooks, because they could clash with the team's own.
  
  ## Repository layout
  
  ├── README.md  ONBOARDING.md  setup.py  VERSION  CHANGELOG.md
  ├── roles/                 role packs: core + one folder per role
  ├── scripts/personal/      the code behind setup.py, its tests and the role-pack validator
  ├── template/              the skill library (.claude/skills) and the retired team-mode skeleton
  ├── scripts/install/       retired team-mode installer (its manifest code is reused)
  └── docs/                  specification, roadmap (design and plan records), visuals, deck
  
  ## Verify locally
  
  pip install "pyyaml>=6"                        # only the validators need it; setup.py does not
  for t in scripts/personal/tests/test_*.py; do python3 "$t"; done
  python3 scripts/personal/validate_packs.py
  
  Kit CI ([`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](./.github/workflows/ci.yml)) also runs every template validator and test, and a `personal-e2e` job: a team repo with its own AI files, the kit copied in, setup, update and remove, ending byte-identical.
  
  ## Retired: team mode
  
  Up to 0.3.x the kit was installed into a repo and committed there (`install.sh`, `scripts/install/`, the CI gates in `template/ci/` and `template/.github/workflows/`). Team mode is retired as of 0.4.0: those files stay in the repo as internal history and reused code, and are no longer offered or supported. See [`CHANGELOG.md`](./CHANGELOG.md).
  
  ## Contributing
  
  Contributions are welcome. See [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](./CONTRIBUTING.md) for the workflow and [`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`](./CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md) for community expectations. Security issues: please follow [`SECURITY.md`](./SECURITY.md) rather than opening a public issue.
  
  ## License
  
  Released under the [MIT License](./LICENSE).
  ```
````

````
  ~~~~markdown
  # AI-SDLC kit: onboarding
  
  > **To Copilot.** The person said "do the onboarding", or "change my preferences", "update the kit", "check the kit" or "remove the kit". Follow the matching section below. Run every command yourself, from the repo root; the person only answers questions. Speak plainly, without git or Python words unless they use them. Never edit a team file: `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, or anything not named `ai-sdlc-*`.
  
  Below, `KIT` is the folder this file is in. Before setup it is wherever the person copied it (for example `tools/ai-sdlc-kit`); after setup it is always `.ai-sdlc/kit`.
  
  ## Do the onboarding
  
  1. **Check Python.** Run `python3 --version`. It must say 3.9 or newer. If the command is missing or older, say: "Your computer needs Python 3.9 or newer before I can set this up. Please ask your IT support to install it." Then stop. Nothing has changed.
  2. **Protect the kit first.** Run `python3 KIT/setup.py setup --protect-only`. It moves the kit to `.ai-sdlc/kit` and hides it from git. From now on, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py`.
  3. **Ask three questions,** one at a time:
     1. "What is your name?"
     2. "What is your role here? You can pick more than one: Product Owner (`po`), Product Manager (`pm`), Scrum Master / Team Coach in SAFe (`sm`), Developer (`dev`), QA (`qa`), Architect (`architect`), Engineering Manager (`em`)."
     3. "Which language should I answer in: English (`en`), Romanian (`ro`) or German (`de`)?"
  
     From the answer to question 3 on, speak that language.
  4. **Set up.** Run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py setup --name "<name>" --roles <ids, comma-separated> --lang <code>`.
  5. **Relay the result** in plain words: who it is set up for, then each item under "Check", with its id in brackets.
  6. **Look for contradictions.** For each `team-…` warning, read the team file it names and the kit's `.github/instructions/ai-sdlc-*.instructions.md`. Tell the person only about real contradictions (one says do X, the other says don't), one sentence each, naming both files. The team's rule wins; say so.
  7. **Acknowledge.** If `setup.py` answers that this command is not built yet, tell the person in their language that it arrives in the next kit version, and stop.
     For each warning the person has understood, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py ack <warning-id>`. It comes back only if that team file changes.
  8. **Close.** Say: "You're set up. Say 'change my preferences', 'update the kit' or 'remove the kit' at any time."
  
  ## Change my preferences
  
  If `setup.py` answers that this command is not built yet, tell the person in their language that it arrives in the next kit version, and stop.
  
  Ask what they want to change, then run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change` with the matching option:
  
  | They want | Option |
  |---|---|
  | another name | `--name "<name>"` |
  | other roles | `--roles <ids>` (the full new list) |
  | another language | `--lang <en, ro or de>` |
  | git done for them, or not | `--git-comfort <hidden, guided, git-native or default>` |
  | the one-line session summary on or off (the session-start check always runs) | `--rituals <status, none or default>` |
  | an extra skill | `--add-skill <skill>` |
  | a skill left out | `--drop-skill <skill>` |
  
  Relay the summary. If it says it kept their edit, explain that the kit's newer copy is next to their file as `<file>.kit-new`, for them to compare.
  
  ## Update the kit
  
  If `setup.py` answers that this command is not built yet, tell the person in their language that it arrives in the next kit version, and stop.
  
  The person copied a newer kit folder into the repo; `check` names it (`kit-copy:<folder>`). Run `python3 <that folder>/setup.py update`. Relay the summary, including any kept edits.
  
  ## Check the kit
  
  Run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check` and relay each item:
  
  - `missing:` or `unexcluded:`: run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change` with no options. It puts files back and hides them again.
  - `unknown:`: a file named like the kit's that the kit did not write. Ask before deleting it.
  - `kit-copy:`: a newer copy means "update the kit"; an older one can be deleted, after asking.
  - `stale-kit`: run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py update`.
  - `team-…` and `skill-clash:…`: as in steps 6 and 7 above.
  
  ## Remove the kit
  
  If `setup.py` answers that this command is not built yet, tell the person in their language that it arrives in the next kit version, and stop.
  
  Ask first: "This removes the kit and your settings from this repo. Files you edited are kept. Continue?" On yes, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py remove` and relay what it removed and kept.
  ~~~~
````

````
  ---
  name: playbook-sm
  description: The Scrum Master / Team Coach seat's role contract in a SAFe setting — what it owns end-to-end, co-owns and with whom, deliberately doesn't touch, and how it works with the other seats and with AI. Invoke whenever someone wants to reason from, act as, or get the Scrum Master's perspective, or to settle a "who owns / who decides" question about team events, PI Planning preparation, PI objectives, team flow, impediments, ART Sync / Scrum of Scrums, Inspect & Adapt, or coaching the team.
  metadata:
    seat: "SM"
    status: "draft"
    classification: "internal"
    ai-trust: "working"
    owner: "Architect"
  ---
  
  # Playbook: Scrum Master / Team Coach
  
  This is the role-seat contract for the **Scrum Master / Team Coach** seat on `<PROJECT_NAME>`, framed by SAFe (Scaled Agile Framework). The seat is a servant leader and coach: it helps the team deliver value in a steady flow, improve how it works, and plan and align with the rest of its Agile Release Train (ART).
  
  ## §1 — Mandate
  
  ### 1.1 Owns end-to-end (sole decision)
  
  1. Facilitating the team events: iteration planning, daily stand-up, iteration review, iteration retrospective, and backlog refinement when the team asks for it.
  2. The team's impediment log: every impediment has an owner, an age and a next step, and is escalated when the team cannot remove it.
  3. Visibility of team flow: the team board, work-in-progress limits, and flow measures with their definition, source and period.
  4. Preparing the team for PI Planning and supporting it during the event: capacity, the team's draft plan, risks and dependencies.
  5. Representing the team at the ART Sync / Scrum of Scrums: progress towards PI objectives, dependencies, impediments that need the train's help.
  6. Coaching the team in its agreed agile practices, and running improvement items through to done.
  
  ### 1.2 Co-owns (with named partner)
  
  | Item | Co-owner | Meaning |
  |------|----------|---------|
  | Team PI objectives | Product Owner + team | The team drafts and commits; the Product Owner brings business value; the SM facilitates and checks they are specific, measurable and owned. |
  | Inspect & Adapt participation | Release Train Engineer | The RTE runs the event; the SM brings the team's evidence and drives the team's improvement items. |
  | Definition of Done | Team + QA | The team agrees it; the SM keeps it visible and used. |
  | Risk handling (ROAM) | Team + RTE | The SM prepares the risks; the team and the train decide resolved, owned, accepted or mitigated. |
  
  ### 1.3 Deliberately doesn't touch
  
  - Backlog content and priority — **Product Owner**.
  - Technical design and the architecture — **Developers** and **Architect**.
  - People management, appraisals and individual performance — out of this seat entirely.
  - Commitments on the team's behalf: the team commits, the SM facilitates.
  
  ## §2 — Decision-rights cheat sheet
  
  | # | Decision | Owner | Consulted | Informed | Escalation trigger |
  |---|----------|-------|-----------|----------|--------------------|
  | 1 | Format and agenda of a team event | SM | Team | Product Owner | The event repeatedly misses its objective |
  | 2 | Escalate an impediment to the ART | SM | Team | RTE | The team cannot remove it within the iteration |
  | 3 | Team PI objectives and their business value | Team + Product Owner | SM | Business owners | Objectives exceed the team's capacity |
  | 4 | Which improvement items the team takes on | Team | SM | RTE | An item needs another team or the train |
  | 5 | Work-in-progress limits on the team board | Team | SM | Product Owner | Flow measures worsen for two iterations |
  
  ## §3 — Working with other seats
  
  **SM ↔ Product Owner** — Together they keep the backlog ready for planning; the SM protects the team's capacity and focus, the Product Owner decides what comes first.
  
  **SM ↔ Team** — The SM serves the team: removes impediments, facilitates, coaches self-organisation, and never assigns work to individuals.
  
  **SM ↔ Release Train Engineer** — The SM brings the team's view to the ART Sync / Scrum of Scrums, PI Planning and Inspect & Adapt, and takes the train's decisions back to the team.
  
  **SM ↔ Engineering Manager / Architect** — Technical impediments and enabler work are raised early, so they reach planning with an owner.
  
  **Escalation** — An impediment the team and the SM cannot remove goes to the RTE; if the train cannot remove it either, to the `<DIRECTOR / SPONSOR>`.
  
  ## §4 — Working with AI (Roles × Skills × MCP)
  
  Ties to the board's Roles × Skills × MCP matrix. See [`AGENTS.md`](../../../AGENTS.md) and [`WORKING-AGREEMENT.md`](../../../WORKING-AGREEMENT.md). AI is a `working`-trust collaborator for this seat: it prepares, the people decide.
  
  - **Invokable skills** — this playbook; `playbook-product` for backlog questions; `skill-creator` to capture a reusable facilitation pattern.
  - **What AI prepares** — in personal setup, the Scrum Master instructions (`ai-sdlc-sm.instructions.md`) list it item by item; this playbook says who owns and decides. In short: flow and impediment signals, event and PI Planning preparation, Inspect & Adapt evidence and ROAM drafts.
  - **Hard line** — signals about the work and the team, never judgements about individual people: no scores, rankings or guesses about anyone's motives, mood or ability.
  - **Evidence, not verdicts** — every number has a definition, a source and a period; no cause-and-effect claim without them.
  - **Scoped writes only** — AI drafts; the SM or the team publishes. It never changes the board, commits a PI objective or closes an impediment on its own.
  
  ## §5 — Definition of done for this seat's artefacts
  
  - Every impediment has an owner, an age and a next step; escalated ones name where they went.
  - PI objectives are specific, measurable, owned and linked to features, with business value set by the business owners.
  - Flow measures state their definition, source and period.
  - Retrospective and Inspect & Adapt actions have an owner and a review date, and are followed up.
  - Event outputs (decisions, risks, actions) are recorded where the team keeps them, per [`WORKING-AGREEMENT.md`](../../../WORKING-AGREEMENT.md).
  ```
  
  ### Final `roles/sm/instructions.md`
  This is the Task 5a text with one sentence added to the first paragraph:
  ```
  # Role: Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe)
  
  You support a Scrum Master / Team Coach on a SAFe Agile Release Train (ART): team flow, impediments, team events, PI Planning and the team's improvement. Say "iteration"; "sprint" means the same. The skill `ai-sdlc-playbook-sm` holds the full role contract; use it for "who owns or decides this" questions.
  
  **What you help with**
  - **Flow health.** Find work that waits: a long time in one status, waiting for review, clarification or testing, blockers without an owner, items moving backward or reopened, work near iteration end without evidence.
  - **Progress evidence.** Compare reported progress with workflow and engineering evidence: commits, reviews, builds, tests.
  - **Impediments and dependencies.** List each with its owner, its age and the next step. For a dependency on another team, name the providing team, the consuming team, what is needed and by when.
  - **Daily stand-up prep.** What changed since yesterday, new blockers, items at risk.
  - **Iteration review pack.** The evidence for what was done, per item; what was not done, and scope changes.
  - **Retrospectives.** Patterns at team level across iterations.
  - **Flow metrics.** Explain cycle time, throughput and work in progress, with the definition, source and period of every number.
  - **Iteration planning, risks and agendas.** Check planning readiness, prepare risks for the team's discussion, and draft agendas with an objective, participants, inputs, timeboxes, decision points and the expected output.
  - **PI Planning prep.** Gather the team's capacity, the draft PI objectives (committed and uncommitted), and the dependencies and risks for the program board.
  - **PI objectives tracking.** List the evidence found per objective, next to the business value the business owners assigned. Never assign it yourself.
  - **ART Sync / Scrum of Scrums prep.** Cross-team dependencies, progress towards PI objectives, and impediments the team cannot remove that need escalating.
  - **System demo and Inspect & Adapt prep.** Collect the team's evidence for the system demo, and the inputs to the problem-solving workshop: observed outcomes, flow and quality signals, recurring problems and candidate improvement items, each with its evidence and its limits.
  - **ROAM.** Prepare PI risks for the ROAM discussion (Resolved, Owned, Accepted, Mitigated). Suggest a category at most: the team and the Release Train Engineer (RTE) decide.
  
  **Limits**
  - Show signals, not judgements about people or performance. Never score individuals.
  - No guesses about interpersonal problems, emotions or ability.
  - No cause-and-effect claims without a source, a definition and a period.
  - Suggest a risk classification at most: the team decides it.
  - Never assign business value or cast confidence votes for the team: business owners assign business value, and the team votes.
  ```

  ## Task 6a: the placement engine. Commit aa2c8e0
  **Pre-checks:** `place.py` did not exist yet. Every API the plan's code calls is present: packs, paths, reuse/manifest, state.new and the helpers (make_repo, snapshot, copy_kit, git). `setup.py` and `VERSION` are at the kit root.
  
  **Expected failure:** as the plan says, `ImportError: cannot import name 'place' from 'personal'`.
  
  **Implementation:** `scripts/personal/place.py` and `test_place.py` are verbatim from the plan. `validate_packs.py` got the plan's import and loop change.
  
  **Test tails** (identical on 3.13.5 and 3.9.6):
  - test_cli 10
  - test_exclude 11
  - test_packs 16 (skipped=1 on 3.9, the PyYAML gap again)
  - test_place 17, which matches the hand-worked count
  - test_roles 7
  - test_state 6
  
  All OK.
  
  **Validators:**
  - `validate_packs.py` on python3: `ok    8 role pack(s) valid; their skills pass validate-skills`. On /usr/bin/python3 it stops with "PyYAML is required", which is expected for this CI-only tool.
  - validate-skills: 8/8.
  
  **Regression run:** all Phase 0 suites at their expected counts, OK.
  
  **Local CI:** first 41 of 43 commands, ALL-GREEN.
  
  **Commit stat:** 4 files, +395/−6: `place.py` +184, `test_place.py` +203, `validate_packs.py` 10, plan 4.
  
  **Plan clarification:** the plan said "20 links in all today", which no longer holds after 5c. It now says "20 before Task 5c and 23 with `playbook-sm`, re-checked: 23 rewritten, 0 missing", and notes the 17-test result as confirmed.
  
  **What the placement engine does:**
  - **One reconcile for every command.** `apply(root, state, wanted)` serves setup, change, update and remove. For each file the choices call for, `manifest.state` decides what happens:
    - NEW, MISSING or CLEAN: write it (an atomic write, recorded with its fingerprint, and any new folders noted in `created_dirs`).
    - IDENTICAL: just record it. This is how an interrupted run finishes without writing duplicates.
    - MODIFIED: keep the person's edit. Only when the kit's own text changed is the kit copy written next to it as `<file>.kit-new`.
    - FOREIGN: leave it alone and report it.
  - **Tracked paths are never touched.** A path git tracks is never written or deleted.
  - **Sweep.** A placed file that is no longer wanted is deleted while unedited. Once edited, it is kept, reported and dropped from state. Empty folders that setup created are pruned, deepest first.
  - **What gets placed.** `wanted_files` builds the core and role instruction files, the placed skills and `USER.md`.
  - **Moving the kit.** `move_kit` uses `shutil.move`, so it renames within one filesystem and copies then deletes across two. It does nothing if the kit is already at `.ai-sdlc/kit`, raises `ValueError` for a kit outside the repo, and raises `FileExistsError` if a second kit is already there.
  - **Link rewrite.** `rewrite_links` is a pure function. A relative link that leaves the skill's folder is pointed at the same file inside `.ai-sdlc/kit/`, relative to where the file is placed. Links inside the folder, URLs, root-absolute paths and `#anchors` are left alone; an anchor after a rewritten path is kept. A link with no target in the kit is left as it is and reported, and `validate_packs.py` now fails CI on such a link.
````

````
  # AI-SDLC: working with Ana
  
  **Language.** Always answer in German (Deutsch), whatever language the question is in. Keep code, commands, file names and quoted text as they are.
  
  **Setup gate.** If `.ai-sdlc/USER.md` is missing, do the onboarding first: follow `.ai-sdlc/kit/ONBOARDING.md`.
  
  **Roles.** Ana works here as: Product Owner, Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe). Each role has its own `ai-sdlc-<role>.instructions.md`. When a reply acts for one role, say which.
  
  **Git.** Do git for them in plain words ("Saved."), without git jargon. Ask before every commit and push. Never commit to main, master or another protected branch; use a personal branch.
  
  **Session.** At the start of each session, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check --quiet` once and mention any warning it prints, in the person's language. The command is read-only. After the check, give a one-line summary of where the work stands.
  
  **A human validates everything.**
  - You suggest; Ana decides. Never approve, sign off, prioritise or close anything for them.
  - Show every change before it is saved, and every command before it changes something.
  - Report "evidence found" or "evidence not found", never "compliant" or "done".
  - No judgements about individual people: talk about the work, the flow and the team.
````

```
  - **playbook-product:32** (co-own table). "Product proposes the pull; EM states what the team can take; the team commits." → "…EM states the team's capacity; the team commits the scope." It used the same "what the team can take" wording as line 59, which makes the EM decide scope.
  - **playbook-product:52** (decision row 5, escalation trigger column). "Product and EM cannot converge on the pull" → "The team cannot converge on a pull with Product and EM". The row's owner is the Team, but the trigger treated it as a Product–EM deal.
  
  The escalation path follows playbook-sm: the RTE at train level, then the director or sponsor. For story-level scope in the dev playbook, the path is the team, then Product.
  
  **Lines I considered and left:**
  - **product:20, :48, :50:** Product owns "Scope / MVP definition", "Add / drop a scope item" and priority. These are about backlog content, which is Product's in SAFe, not about committing to an iteration or PI.
  - **product:24, :51:** Product owns milestones, roadmap dates and the delivery plan. That is roadmap and dates, not the team's iteration commitment.
  - **em:21, :30, :45, :55:** these already say the EM commits capacity or velocity and the team commits scope.
  - **em:49 and the veto in em:55:** the EM and Product jointly approve, and either can veto, a tech-debt iteration. That is prioritisation plus engineering practice, not a scope commitment. Flag it if the owner sees it differently.
  - **dev:35, dev:51, qa:41:** already fixed by the previous pass, and correct.
  - **architect:34, :56, :66:** these cover velocity mechanics (EM), cost and risk that shape scope (no decision right), and general cross-seat disputes.

  - **sm:** reference only, unchanged.
```

````
  ## AI-SDLC Bootstrap Kit 0.4.0: a personal AI setup for everyone on the team
  
  **What it is.** The kit gives each person on a software team (product owners, product managers, scrum masters, developers, QA, architects, engineering managers) GitHub Copilot instructions and skills that fit their role, their language and how they like to work, inside the shared repo, without committing anything to it.
  
  ### Set it up
  1. Copy the kit folder anywhere into your repo.
  2. Open Copilot Chat in Agent mode (or run `copilot`) and say **"do the onboarding"**.
  3. Answer three questions: your name, your role(s), your language (English, Romanian or German).
  
  Later, say "change my preferences", "update the kit", "check the kit" or "remove the kit". You need Python 3.9 or newer; Copilot checks it first.
  
  ### Roles
  Product Owner, Product Manager, Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe), Developer, QA, Architect and Engineering Manager. Each has its own instructions and playbook skill. You can hold several roles.
  
  ### Eight skills for every role
  - **Documents:** Word, Excel, PowerPoint and PDF (`doc-word`, `doc-excel`, `doc-powerpoint`, `doc-pdf`). Their libraries are installed only with your consent, into `~/.ai-sdlc/venv`, outside the repo.
  - **Visuals:** draw.io diagrams (`drawio`), HTML explainers (`visual-explainers`), and issues or PRs with a Mermaid diagram for GitHub, Bitbucket or Jira (`visual-issue`).
  - **Memory:** `deceneus` suggests what to remember from a chat and saves only what you approve, to your own hidden files.
  
  ### Safety
  - **Nothing is committed.** Every file the kit adds is hidden from git through `.git/info/exclude`; `git status` stays clean.
  - **Team files are untouched.** The kit never creates or edits `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md` or `.vscode/settings.json`. If the team's AI files overlap the kit's, you get a warning, and where they disagree the team's rule wins.
  - **A human validates everything** the AI writes or decides. Every role works under this rule.
  - No network use, no git command that changes anything, and `remove` leaves the repo byte for byte as it was. Files you edited are kept.
  
  ### Upgrading from 0.3
  **Team mode is retired.** `install.sh` no longer installs and the CI gates are no longer offered; they stay in the repo as internal history. To move to 0.4.0, each person copies the kit in and says "do the onboarding". Files team mode already committed are left alone; the team decides whether to remove them. From now on, upgrade by copying a newer kit folder in and saying "update the kit".
  
  Full details: `CHANGELOG.md` in the kit.
  ```
````

````
  Task 14 is done and CI is green on every job and leg. PR #12 is open, not a draft and not merged: https://github.com/danemil/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/pull/12
  
  There is one CI-config risk that I found after the green run. As instructed, I did not push a fix:
  - **The 3.9 leg will break when `ubuntu-latest` changes.** Every job in the run carries the annotation "The ubuntu-latest label will migrate to Ubuntu 26 beginning October 19, 2026."
  - **3.9 has no Ubuntu 26.04 build.** The `actions/python-versions` manifest lists 3.9.25 only for 22.04 and 24.04 (3.12.15 also has 26.04).
  - **The fix is one line.** Pin the 3.9 leg to `runs-on: ubuntu-24.04`. The plan's Step 5 now says exactly that. Today 3.9 is available on `ubuntu-latest`, which is 24.04, so I did not pin it.
  
  **Commit:** 6d2281a on `ci/personal-e2e`, cut from origin/main 5bb6369. It touches only `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `docs/roadmap/2026-10-08-personal-setup-plan.md`. The grep of the staged diff for other-project names was empty. CHANGELOG.md, VERSION and docs/prompts/** are untouched. `README.md` is unchanged because its "Verify locally" section is still correct.
  
  ## The job, in full
  ```yaml
    # Personal setup end to end, as a person does it: a team repo with its own AI files,
    # the kit copied in, setup as Copilot runs it from ONBOARDING.md, ack, change, an update
    # from a newer copy, then remove, ending byte-identical. Plan: Task 14 in
    # docs/roadmap/2026-10-08-personal-setup-plan.md. Replaces the team-mode adopt-e2e job.
    # No pyyaml here, which proves setup.py is stdlib only. The 3.9 leg runs only this job;
    # the Phase 0 suites (ai-governance, 3.12) need tomllib.
    personal-e2e:
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      strategy:
        fail-fast: false
        matrix:
          # 3.9 is the floor setup.py promises (ONBOARDING.md checks it); 3.12 matches
          # ai-governance. A plain list, no `include` (the H8 trap).
          python: ['3.9', '3.12']
      env:
        PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE: '1'   # no __pycache__ in the checkout the kit is copied from
      defaults:
        run:
          shell: bash
      steps:
        - uses: actions/checkout@v7
        - uses: actions/setup-python@v7
          with:
            python-version: ${{ matrix.python }}
  
        - name: Personal setup unit tests on this Python
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            python3 --version
            for t in scripts/personal/tests/test_*.py; do echo "== $t"; python3 "$t"; done
  
        - name: Throwaway git identity
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            git config --global user.name "kit-ci"
            git config --global user.email "kit-ci@users.noreply.github.com"
            git config --global init.defaultBranch main
  
        - name: A team repo with its own AI files
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            T="$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            mkdir -p "$T" && cd "$T" && git init -q
            mkdir -p .github/instructions .claude/skills/playbook-product docs
            printf '# Team brief\nUse British English.\n' > AGENTS.md
            printf -- "---\napplyTo: 'docs/**'\n---\nDocs need two reviewers.\n" > .github/instructions/team.instructions.md
            printf -- '---\nname: playbook-product\ndescription: The team playbook.\n---\nTeam rules.\n' > .claude/skills/playbook-product/SKILL.md
            printf 'Hello.\n' > docs/readme.md
            git add -A && git commit -qm "team repo"
            for f in $(git ls-files); do echo "$(git hash-object "$f") $f"; done > "$RUNNER_TEMP/team-hashes.txt"
            python3 "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/scripts/personal/tests/helpers.py" snapshot "$T" > "$RUNNER_TEMP/before.json"
  
        - name: Copy the kit in and set up, as Copilot does from ONBOARDING.md
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            T="$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            mkdir "$T/ai-sdlc-kit"
            tar -C "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE" --exclude=.git -cf - . | tar -xf - -C "$T/ai-sdlc-kit"
            cd "$T"
            test ! -e ai-sdlc-kit/.git && test -f ai-sdlc-kit/setup.py
            python3 ai-sdlc-kit/setup.py setup --protect-only
            test ! -e ai-sdlc-kit && test -f .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py
            out="$(python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py setup --name "CI Person" --roles po,sm --lang de)"
            printf '%s\n' "$out"
            test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"            # nothing for git to see
            git diff --exit-code HEAD                      # team files unchanged ...
            for f in $(git ls-files); do echo "$(git hash-object "$f") $f"; done \
              | diff "$RUNNER_TEMP/team-hashes.txt" -     # ... byte for byte
            for id in team-agents-md 'team-instructions:.github/instructions/team.instructions.md' \
                      'skill-clash:.claude/skills/playbook-product'; do
              printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF "[$id]" || { echo "missing warning $id"; exit 1; }
            done
            grep -q 'Always answer in German' .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-core.instructions.md
            test -f .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-sm.instructions.md
            test -f .agents/skills/ai-sdlc-playbook-product/SKILL.md
            test -f .agents/skills/ai-sdlc-playbook-sm/SKILL.md
            # Core skills are placed as whole folders (references included).
            test -f .agents/skills/ai-sdlc-drawio/references/xml-reference.md
  
        - name: Acknowledge one warning; it goes quiet, the others stay
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            cd "$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py ack team-agents-md
            out="$(python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check || true)"   # exit 1: there are findings
            printf '%s\n' "$out"
            if printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF '[team-agents-md]'; then echo "ack ignored"; exit 1; fi
            printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF '[skill-clash:.claude/skills/playbook-product]'
            python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check --quiet | grep '^AI-SDLC '
  
        - name: Change a preference; only the core file and USER.md change
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            cd "$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            out="$(python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change --lang en)"
            printf '%s\n' "$out"
            printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF 'Wrote 2 file(s): .ai-sdlc/USER.md, .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-core.instructions.md'
            grep -q 'Always answer in English' .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-core.instructions.md
            test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"
  
        - name: Update from a newer copy; an edited file is kept
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            T="$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            mkdir "$T/ai-sdlc-kit-new"
            tar -C "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE" --exclude=.git -cf - . | tar -xf - -C "$T/ai-sdlc-kit-new"
            cd "$T"
            echo 9.9.9 > ai-sdlc-kit-new/VERSION
            printf '\nNewer kit line.\n' >> ai-sdlc-kit-new/roles/po/instructions.md
            printf '\nMy own note.\n' >> .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md
            out="$(python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check || true)"   # exit 1: there are findings
            printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF '[kit-copy:ai-sdlc-kit-new]'
            python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update
            test "$(cat .ai-sdlc/kit/VERSION)" = 9.9.9 && test ! -e ai-sdlc-kit-new
            grep -q 'My own note' .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md
            if grep -q 'Newer kit line' .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md; then
              echo "edited file overwritten"; exit 1
            fi
            grep -q 'Newer kit line' .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md.kit-new
            test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"
  
        # remove keeps edited files, so the person first takes the kit's copy (`change`
        # records it), then removes. Nothing is left over: both kit copies lived at the repo
        # root and were moved away by setup and update (checked above).
        - name: Take the kit's copy, then remove; the repo is byte-identical to before
          run: |
            set -euo pipefail
            T="$RUNNER_TEMP/team"
            cd "$T"
            mv .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md.kit-new .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md
            python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change
            out="$(python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py remove)"
            printf '%s\n' "$out"
            printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -qF 'The repo is back to how it was before setup.'
            python3 "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/scripts/personal/tests/helpers.py" snapshot "$T" > "$RUNNER_TEMP/after.json"
            diff "$RUNNER_TEMP/before.json" "$RUNNER_TEMP/after.json"
            test -z "$(git status --porcelain)"
  ```
  
  **Changes to `ai-governance`:**
  - One step added after `Installer end-to-end`: `Validate role packs (roles/*, and their skills against agentskills.io)`, which runs `python3 scripts/personal/validate_packs.py`.
  - Every existing command is kept, and the Task 2 personal-suites loop is not duplicated.
````

```
  **Final release-notes text**
  
  ## AI-SDLC Bootstrap Kit 0.4.0: a personal AI setup for everyone on the team
  
  **What it is.** The kit gives each person on a software team (product owners, product managers, scrum masters, developers, QA, architects, engineering managers) GitHub Copilot instructions and skills that fit their role, their language and how they like to work, inside the shared repo, without committing anything to it.
  
  ### Set it up
  1. Copy the kit folder anywhere into your repo.
  2. Open Copilot Chat in Agent mode (or run `copilot`) and say **"do the onboarding"**.
  3. Answer three questions: your name, your role(s), your language (English, Romanian or German).
  
  Later, say "change my preferences", "update the kit", "check the kit" or "remove the kit". You need Python 3.9 or newer; Copilot checks it first.
  
  ### Roles
  Product Owner, Product Manager, Scrum Master / Team Coach (SAFe), Developer, QA, Architect and Engineering Manager. Each has its own instructions and playbook skill. You can hold several roles.
  
  ### Eight skills for every role
  - **Documents:** Word, Excel, PowerPoint and PDF (`doc-word`, `doc-excel`, `doc-powerpoint`, `doc-pdf`). Their libraries are installed only with your consent, into `~/.ai-sdlc/venv`, outside the repo.
  - **Visuals:** draw.io diagrams (`drawio`), HTML explainers (`visual-explainers`), and issues or PRs with a Mermaid diagram for GitHub, Bitbucket or Jira (`visual-issue`).
  - **Memory:** `deceneus` suggests what to remember from a chat and saves only what you approve, to your own hidden files.
  
  ### Safety
  - **Nothing is committed.** Every file the kit adds is hidden from git through `.git/info/exclude`; `git status` stays clean.
  - **Team files are untouched.** The kit never creates or edits `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md` or `.vscode/settings.json`. If the team's AI files overlap the kit's, you get a warning, and where they disagree the team's rule wins.
  - **A human validates everything** the AI writes or decides. Every role works under this rule.
  - Setup itself never uses the network or runs a git command that changes anything, and `remove` leaves the repo byte for byte as it was (files you edited, and your personal notes and skills, are kept). The document skills download their libraries only after you agree.
  
  ### Upgrading from 0.3
  **Team mode is retired.** `install.sh` no longer installs and the CI gates are no longer offered; they stay in the repo as internal history. To move to 0.4.0, each person copies the kit in and says "do the onboarding". Files team mode already committed are left alone; the team decides whether to remove them. From now on, upgrade by copying a newer kit folder in and saying "update the kit".
  
  Full details: `CHANGELOG.md` in the kit.
</agent-message>
```

````
  **Tests: 195 on both Pythons, all passing** (`python3` 3.13.5 and `/usr/bin/python3` 3.9.6). That is 58 new tests in `test_connectors_foundation.py` plus the existing 137, which are unchanged. On 3.9.6, `test_packs` skips 1 test, as it did before. `validate_packs.py` reports 8 packs valid.
  
  Two things to know about the tests:
  - **Not strictly test-first.** I wrote the tests alongside the foundation code rather than before it; the only red→green I actually saw was one wrong assertion in my own test.
  - **TLS, DNS, 407 and timeout errors are only partly tested live.** 401, 403, connection refused, 429/5xx retries and the proxy behaviour (`HTTP_PROXY` used, `NO_PROXY` bypass) run against the fake server. The other four are tested by mapping constructed exceptions to messages, because the stdlib can't make a test certificate.
  
  **Docs**
  - Design: `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/docs/roadmap/2026-10-08-connectors-design.md`
  - Plan: `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/docs/roadmap/2026-10-08-connectors-plan.md`
  - I added one "Superseded and extended" line at the top of §5.0 in `2026-10-07-onboarding-roles-and-skills-design.md`.
  - **Needs the owner:** you didn't give the role → connector defaults, so design §5 has a proposed table for the owner to confirm in Task C. core: none; po and pm: jira, confluence, jama; sm: jira, confluence; dev and em: jira, bitbucket, jenkins, confluence; qa: jira, jama, jenkins, bitbucket; architect: confluence, jira, bitbucket.
  
  **B1–B5 task specs.** Each task creates exactly two files, `scripts/personal/connectors/<name>.py` and `scripts/personal/tests/test_connector_<name>.py`, and modifies nothing. The registry finds connectors by globbing the folder, and CI's existing `test_*.py` loop picks up the new suites. Each agent works on its own branch, `feat/connectors-<name>`, merged back into `feat/connectors`. The plan gives every command's endpoints, paging and exact JSON keys.
  
  | Task | Auth | Commands | Min. tests |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | B1 jira | Cloud: Basic email + token; DC: Bearer | `whoami`, `search <JQL> --limit --fields`, `issue <KEY> --changelog --links`, `sprints <board> --state` | 14 |
  | B2 confluence | as Jira | `whoami`, `page <id> --format text\|storage --max-chars`, `search <CQL or text> --space --limit` | 10 |
  | B3 bitbucket | Bearer HTTP access token | `whoami`, `prs P/r --state --limit`, `pr P/r/id --diff --comments --max-diff-bytes`, `branches P/r --filter --limit` | 11 |
  | B4 jama | OAuth client credentials | `whoami`, `item <id>`, `search <text> --project --type --limit`, `relationships <id> --direction`, `testruns --cycle\|--plan --limit` | 11 |
  | B5 jenkins | Basic username + API token | `whoami`, `job <path>`, `build <path> <n\|last\|lastSuccessful\|lastFailed>`, `tests <path> <ref> --all` | 11 |
  
  - **jira:** Cloud uses `/rest/api/3/search/jql` with a cursor; DC uses `/rest/api/2/search` with offsets. Changelog: Cloud `/issue/{k}/changelog`, DC `expand=changelog`.
  - **confluence:** Cloud reads pages with v2 under `/wiki`; DC uses the content API. CQL search uses the v1 API on both, with link paging.
  - **bitbucket:** `check()` refuses `bitbucket.org`. `whoami` reads the `X-AUSERNAME` header.
  - **jama:** REST v1; item data under `data`, totals under `meta.pageInfo`.
  - **jenkins:** the `/job/a/job/b` path is built from `a/b`; `whoami` treats "anonymous" as a 401.
  - **Confirm on live servers:** Bitbucket's `X-AUSERNAME` header and raw `.diff` endpoint, and Jama's test-run field names. The plan flags these.
  - **Task C** covers the core `connectors` skill, the role defaults, the pack-validation change, the onboarding and summary one-liners, README/CHANGELOG, and a subprocess end-to-end suite plus a CI step. It then opens the PR.
  
  **Foundation API the B agents use**
  ```python
  # registry.py
  Field(key, prompt, secret=[REDACTED:secret], required=True, identity=False, when=None, help="")
  Command(help, run, args=None)            # run(ctx, args) -> Result; args(parser)
  Result(item=None, items=None, truncated=False, lines=None, extra={})
  Context(name, client, values, kind)      # kind "cloud" | "dc" | ""
  discover(extra=()) -> dict[str, Connector];  from_module(name, mod) -> Connector
  open_context(connector, values, **client_kwargs) -> Context;  load_values(connector)
  # module contract: TITLE, FIELDS (first is "url"; ca_bundle is added automatically), auth(values),
  #   optional kind(values) and check(values), WHOAMI (item has user, display_name), COMMANDS
  
  # http.py
  ConnectorError(message, kind="http", status=None)
  bearer(token) / basic(user, secret) / oauth_client_credentials(client_id, client_secret, token_path="/rest/oauth/token")
  Client(base_url, auth=None, *, ca_bundle=None, timeout=30.0, retries=3, backoff=1.0,
         sleep=time.sleep, debug=None, allow_http=False, stderr=None)
    .url(path, params=None)  .web_url(path)  .get(path, params=None, *, accept=...) -> Response
    .get_json(path, params=None)  .get_text(path, params=None, *, accept="text/plain", max_bytes=None) -> (text, truncated)
    .paginate(path, params=None, *, items="values", paging=None, limit=50, page_size=50) -> (items, truncated)
  Offset(start="startAt", size="maxResults", total="total", last=None); BitbucketPaging();
  TokenPaging(key="nextPageToken", param="nextPageToken", size="maxResults"); LinkPaging(key="_links.next", base="_links.base", size="limit")
  dig(data, "a.b", default=None);  atlassian_kind(url) -> "cloud" | "dc"
  
  # text.py
  html_to_text(html, max_chars=None); adf_to_text(node); clip(text, n); iso_from_ms(ms)
  
  # tests/fakeserver.py
  FakeServer(routes), Reply(status, body, headers), Seq(...), free_port()
  ConnectorTestCase: .server(routes) .connector(name) .context(connector, values, kind=None)
                     .run_command(connector, ctx, argv) .run_cli(connector, argv, values=None)
  ```
  
  **Design choices beyond your brief**
  - **HTTPS required:** the client refuses plain `http://` URLs except on localhost/127.0.0.1, and refuses URLs with a user or password in them.
  - **Redirects:** followed only to the same host, so credentials never go elsewhere.
  - **CA bundle:** added on top of the system CAs rather than replacing them. Order: per-connector `ca_bundle`, then `AI_SDLC_CA_BUNDLE`, then `SSL_CERT_FILE`.
  - **Exit codes for `connectors.py`:** 0 ok, 1 error, 2 usage, 3 not connected.
  - **Shared test base:** I added `ConnectorTestCase` and `text.iso_from_ms` now, so the B agents don't each write their own.
  - **Kind in commands:** commands must branch on `ctx.kind`, never the URL; that lets tests force Cloud against the 127.0.0.1 fake server.
  - **Non-terminal `connect`:** when stdin isn't a terminal and every value is in the environment, `connect` saves those values to the file and then runs the test.
  - **No real connector ships yet:** `setup.py connections` and `connectors.py` say "none available in this kit yet".
</agent-message>
````

```
  **Where it is**
  - Worktree: `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/.claude/worktrees/agent-a041fe7634c2be9ce`
  - Branch: `feat/connectors-bitbucket`, cut from `origin/feat/connectors` at 77614c2.
  - Commit: `cf78f09` "feat(connectors): bitbucket Data Center (read-only)", with the Co-Authored-By line.
  - Files: only the two new ones, with 532 lines added in total. No other file was touched and the foundation needed nothing new.
    - `scripts/personal/connectors/bitbucket.py`
    - `scripts/personal/tests/test_connector_bitbucket.py`
  - The forbidden-names grep on the staged diff printed nothing. `__pycache__` folders are removed and the working tree is clean.
  
  **Commands** (run as `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py bitbucket <cmd> ...`)
  - `whoami --json` → `{user, display_name, email|null, url: <base>/users/<slug>}`
    - It sends `GET /rest/api/1.0/projects?limit=1` and reads the `X-AUSERNAME` header. If that header is missing, it fails as "unauthorized" with "Bitbucket did not say who you are".
    - It then calls `GET /rest/api/1.0/users/{slug}`; a 404 there is tolerated, and `display_name` falls back to the slug.
  - `prs PRJ/web --state ALL --limit 10 --json` → items `{id, title, state, author, reviewers:[{name,status}], from_branch, to_branch, created, updated, url}`
    - Request: `GET .../pull-requests?state=&order=NEWEST`, paged with `BitbucketPaging`. Default state is OPEN, default limit 25, cap 1000.
    - `url` is the PR's `links.self[0].href`.
  - `pr PRJ/web/42 --diff --comments --max-diff-bytes 50000 --json` → the `prs` fields plus `description` (null if absent).
    - `--diff` adds `diff` and `diff_truncated`, from `GET .../pull-requests/42.diff` with Accept `text/plain`, clipped at `--max-diff-bytes` (default 200000).
    - `--comments` adds `comments: [{id, author, text, created, path|null, line|null, url: <pr url>/overview?commentId=<id>}]`. It reads `.../activities` (up to 500) and keeps only `action == COMMENTED`.
    - I also added a `comments_truncated` key, which the plan does not list. Drop it if you want the exact spec shape.
  - `branches PRJ/web --filter feat --limit 20 --json` → items `{name, id, latest_commit, is_default, url: <base>/projects/P/repos/r/browse?at=refs%2Fheads%2F...}`
    - Request: `GET .../branches?filterText=&orderBy=MODIFICATION`. Default limit is 50.
  
  **Other behaviour**
  - Authentication is `Bearer <token>` and the kind is always `"dc"`.
  - `check()` refuses `bitbucket.org` and any of its subdomains, with the plan's exact message. A host like `bitbucket.org.example.com` is accepted.
  - One helper parses `<project>/<repo>[/<id>]`. A bad shape or a non-numeric id raises a "config" error that shows the expected form.
  - Path segments are URL-quoted and times are converted with `text.iso_from_ms`.
  
  **Tests**
  - New suite: 18 tests (the plan's minimum is 11), all passing on python3 and on `/usr/bin/python3` (3.9.6).
  - Every personal suite passes on both Pythons, including the foundation's 58 tests. The only difference is one test skipped in `test_packs` on 3.9.6.
  - `validate_packs.py` passes: 8 role packs valid.
  
  **Needs a live server to confirm** (also noted in the test file's docstring; tests cover both only against the fake server)
  1. That Bitbucket DC sends the `X-AUSERNAME` response header when the request uses an HTTP access token (personal, project or repository). `whoami`, and so `setup.py connect --test`, depend on it.
  2. That the raw diff endpoint `GET .../pull-requests/{id}.diff` exists (Bitbucket DC 6.7+).
  3. Less critical: the comment line number is read from `commentAnchor.path/line` on the activity, with `comment.anchor` as a fallback. Worth checking on a real line comment.
</agent-message>
```
