```
  Task B5 (jenkins) is finished and committed. Nothing was pushed. The new suite and every personal suite pass on both Pythons.
  
  **Where it is**
  - Worktree: `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/.claude/worktrees/agent-ab172fc77f4f27e1e`
  - Branch: `feat/connectors-jenkins`, cut from `origin/feat/connectors` at 77614c2
  - Commit: `c0188cb feat(connectors): jenkins (read-only)`, ending with the Co-Authored-By line
  - It touches only these two files (656 lines added):
    - `scripts/personal/connectors/jenkins.py`
    - `scripts/personal/tests/test_connector_jenkins.py`
  - The foundation had everything needed, so no other file was changed.
  - The forbidden-names grep on the staged diff printed nothing. `validate_packs.py` passed (8 role packs valid). `__pycache__` folders were removed.
  
  **Test counts**
  - `test_connector_jenkins.py`: 19 tests, OK on python3 and on /usr/bin/python3 (3.9.6). The plan asks for at least 11.
  - All 16 personal suites are OK on both Pythons. `test_packs.py` reports "OK (skipped=1)" on 3.9.6. That suite is not part of this change, so I take the skip to be existing behaviour, but I did not check it against the foundation.
  - The tests were written first and failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: personal.connectors.jenkins` before the module existed.
  
  **Commands** (the tool only sends GET requests to `/api/json` endpoints with `tree=`; the `tree` values are exactly the ones in the plan)
  - `connectors.py jenkins whoami --json`
    - Calls `GET /me/api/json?tree=id,fullName`.
    - Returns `{"user": "ana", "display_name": "Ana Example", "url": "<base>/user/ana"}`.
    - If Jenkins answers "anonymous" it raises `ConnectorError(kind="unauthorized")`, and the CLI then prints the `setup.py connect jenkins` hint.
  - `connectors.py jenkins job team/app/main --json`
    - Calls `GET /job/team/job/app/job/main/api/json?tree=...`.
    - Returns `{name, full_name, status, building, health:[{score, description}], last_build:{number, result, at, url}|null, last_success:{number, url}|null, last_failure:{number, url}|null, children:[{name, status, url}], url}`.
  - `connectors.py jenkins build team/app/main lastSuccessful --json`
    - Calls `GET .../lastSuccessfulBuild/api/json?tree=...`.
    - Returns `{number, display_name, result, building, started, duration_s, causes[], parameters:[{name, value}], changes:[{commit, message, author}], url}`.
    - Password parameters (any `_class` containing "Password") are dropped.
    - Changes come from both `changeSet` (freestyle jobs) and `changeSets` (pipelines), with duplicates removed.
  - `connectors.py jenkins tests team/app/main last [--all] --json`
    - Calls `GET .../lastBuild/testReport/api/json?tree=...`.
    - Returns `{fail, pass, skip, duration_s, cases:[{class, name, status, duration_s, error}], url: ".../testReport/"}`.
    - By default only FAILED and REGRESSION cases are listed; `--all` lists every case. Errors are clipped to 2000 characters.
    - A 404 raises not_found with "Build <ref> of <path> has no test report."
  
  **Behaviour you might not assume**
  - **Job paths:** `a/b`, `job/a/job/b` and `/a/b/` all become `/job/a/job/b`. Each segment is unquoted, then quoted with `safe=""`, so an already-encoded `%2F` is not double-encoded.
  - **Build refs:** a number, `last`, `lastSuccessful` or `lastFailed`. The Jenkins names themselves (`lastBuild` and so on) are also accepted. Anything else is an argparse usage error (exit 2) and no request is sent.
  - **Status from colour:** `blue`, `red`, `yellow`, `aborted`, `disabled` and `notbuilt` map to the plan's words, and an `_anime` suffix sets `building: true`. A folder has no colour, so its status is null. An unknown colour is passed through as it is.
  - **Item URLs:** the server's own `url` is used when present, otherwise one is built with `web_url`. The test-report URL is always built: the job path, the ref, then `testReport/`.
  - **Durations:** build durations come in milliseconds and are converted to seconds; test-report durations are already seconds.
  - **Plain text:** `job`, `build` and `tests` have their own `lines`; `whoami` uses the generic form.
  
  **Not covered and needs a decision**
  - The plan's "paging across two pages" check does not apply: Jenkins `/api/json` with `tree=` returns everything in one response, so these commands have no `--limit` or `truncated`. A large `tests --all` report or a big folder is therefore returned in full. The fix would be a range such as `jobs[...]{0,N}`, but I left it out to stay within the spec.
  
  **Needs live confirmation on a real Jenkins**
  1. A real API token with Basic auth returns the user's id from `/me/api/json`, and a wrong token gives "anonymous" or a 401 (both are handled).
  2. Jenkins accepts the URL-encoded `tree=` value, with its brackets and commas.
  3. The server's own `url` fields point at a host people can open. If Jenkins' root URL is misconfigured or it sits behind a proxy, item links could point at the wrong host.
  4. Multi-configuration (matrix) and some pipeline test reports return `childReports` instead of `suites`. These are not handled: such builds would show counts but no cases.
  5. Password parameter values are dropped whatever Jenkins returns for them; the real response shape for password parameters is not yet confirmed.
</agent-message>
```

```
  Task B2 (the confluence connector) is done and committed locally. It is not pushed.
  
  **Where it is**
  - Worktree: `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/.claude/worktrees/agent-a90bd13177d013de0`
  - Branch: `feat/connectors-confluence`, cut from `origin/feat/connectors` at 77614c2.
  - Commit: `01526f9 feat(connectors): confluence (Cloud and Data Center, read-only)`, ending with the Co-Authored-By line.
  - Two files added, nothing else changed:
    - `scripts/personal/connectors/confluence.py` (188 lines)
    - `scripts/personal/tests/test_connector_confluence.py` (331 lines)
  - The forbidden-names grep on the staged diff found nothing. `__pycache__` folders are removed.
  
  **Commands** (the registry finds the module; `connectors.py` lists `confluence: whoami, page, search`)
  - `connectors.py confluence whoami --json` returns an item with `{user, display_name, email (or null), kind, url}`. `url` is the site root, with `/wiki` on Cloud.
  - `connectors.py confluence page 123456 --json`, with `--format text|storage` (default text) and `--max-chars` (default 20000), returns `{id, title, space, version, updated, body, body_truncated, url}`. The text body comes from `text.html_to_text`. There is also a plain-text form: a title/url/space/version line, then the body.
  - `connectors.py confluence search "release notes" --space ENG --limit 25 --json` returns items with `{id, type, title, space, updated, excerpt, url}`. The JSON envelope also has `"cql"`, so the reader sees the query that actually ran.
  - `connectors.py confluence search 'type = page AND label = "adr"' --json` passes the CQL through unchanged.
  
  **Cloud and Data Center differences handled**
  - **Kind and auth:** kind comes from `http.atlassian_kind`. Cloud uses Basic with email and token; Data Center uses Bearer with a PAT. The `email` field is asked only on Cloud (`when=`).
  - **API root:** one helper, `_root(ctx)`. On Cloud it is `/wiki` unless the saved URL already ends in `/wiki`. On Data Center it is empty, so a context path like `/confluence` in the saved URL is kept. Both cases are tested.
  - **whoami:** reads `{root}/rest/api/user/current`. The user is `accountId` on Cloud and `username` on Data Center. A `type: anonymous` answer raises `ConnectorError(kind="unauthorized")`.
  - **page:**
    - Cloud: `GET {root}/api/v2/pages/{id}?body-format=storage`. Space is `spaceId`; updated is `version.createdAt`.
    - Data Center: `GET /rest/api/content/{id}?expand=body.storage,version,space`. Space is `space.key`; updated is `version.when`.
  - **Page URL:** `_links.base` + `_links.webui`. Without a base, it falls back to the site root (plus `/wiki` on Cloud).
  - **search:**
    - Cloud: `{root}/rest/api/search`. Hits come from `results[].content`; updated is `lastModified`. The excerpt has the `@@@hl@@@` markers removed, goes through `html_to_text`, and is clipped to 300 characters.
    - Data Center: `/rest/api/content/search?expand=space,version`. The excerpt is `null`.
    - Both use `LinkPaging()` with `results` as the items key and `limit` as the page size.
  - **Query building:** `build_cql`. A query counts as CQL when it contains `=`, `~`, ` AND `, ` OR `, ` ORDER BY ` or ` IN (` (case-insensitive). Otherwise it becomes `text ~ "<escaped>" AND type = page`, escaping both `"` and `\`.
  
  **Tests:** 16 in the new suite (the plan asks for at least 10), all passing on python3 3.13.5 and /usr/bin/python3 3.9.6. All 16 personal suites pass on both Pythons; the foundation suite is still 58/58. `validate_packs.py` says ok (8 packs).
  
  **Where the plan was wrong or I deviated**
  1. **Forcing `kind="cloud"` does not switch auth in tests.** `registry.open_context` picks auth from the URL when it builds the client, so a 127.0.0.1 URL always gets Bearer. To show Basic on a recorded request, the Cloud tests replace `ctx.client.auth` with the auth a `*.atlassian.net` URL gives. There is also a direct `auth()` unit test. Jira (B1) will hit the same issue. It is not a foundation bug, but the plan's "tests force kind=cloud" only covers paths, not auth.
  2. **`--space` with user CQL:** the plan says to append `AND space = "KEY"`. Appending it as-is breaks the meaning of an `A OR B` query and breaks any query with `ORDER BY`. I wrap the user's CQL instead: `(cql) AND space = "KEY"`, placed before any `ORDER BY`.
  3. **Cloud search adds `expand=content.space,content.version`:** the plan lists only `cql` and `limit`. Without the expand, Cloud hits have no space key.
  4. **Body clipping:** `body_truncated` is a plain cut at `--max-chars`, without the `…` that `text.clip` adds, so the length is exact.
  5. **Plain-text output:** in plain text (no `--json`), search results come out in the generic form, so the CQL that ran is visible only in the JSON envelope.
  
  **Needs confirming on a live server**
  - Cloud `/wiki/rest/api/search` accepting `expand=content.space,content.version`.
  - The `@@@hl@@@` excerpt markers.
  - That `_links.next` on both Cloud and Data Center is relative to `_links.base`, which includes the context path.
  - Whether Data Center's `user/current` returns `email` (the connector assumes it is usually null).
  
  The first two are also noted in the test file's docstring.
</agent-message>
```

```
  - **Commands:** each command's request and output keys are checked. Search paging is tested across two pages (startAt 0/50, maxResults 50/20) with `truncated`. A missing item gives a 404 (`not_found`).
  
  **Choices that differ from the plan or need your review**
  1. **Relationships paging:** `relationships` pages with its own small loop over `ctx.client.get_json` and `http.Offset`, instead of `Client.paginate`. `paginate` drops each page's `linked.items`, which is where the item names come from. It is still GET only.
  2. **`include` parameter:** it is sent repeated (`include=data.fromItem&include=data.toItem`), which is how Jama's Swagger defines an array. The plan wrote it comma-separated.
  3. **Explicit first page:** `startAt=0` is sent on the first page, so every request is explicit.
  4. **Relationship ends without names:** if an end item is missing from `linked.items`, its key and name are null and its URL has no `?projectId`.
  5. **Truncation at the limit:**
     - `relationships`: when the upstream side fills `--limit`, downstream is not read and the result is marked truncated.
     - `testruns --plan`: when the limit is reached with cycles still unread, the result is marked truncated.
  
  **Needs confirming on a live instance** (also noted in the test file's docstring)
  - **Test-run field names:** `fields.testRunStatus`, `testCase`, `testCycle`, `executionDate` and `assignedTo`. Jama lets each organization rename these.
  - **Item URL:** `<base>/perspective.req#/items/<id>?projectId=<p>`. Jama's support pages give this form, though they also say UI links are not guaranteed to stay stable. Test runs use the same item form, and Jama's UI may have a separate view for them.
  - **Other field names:**
    - `linked.items` should be keyed by the item id as a string.
    - The plan-to-cycles endpoint is `/rest/v1/testplans/{id}/testcycles`.
    - `users/current` should return `username`, `firstName`, `lastName` and `email`.
  
  **Sources**
  - [Accessing Jama Items via Direct URLs](https://support.jamasoftware.com/hc/en-us/articles/42485802990861-Accessing-Jama-Items-via-Direct-URLs)
  - [Velocity API Help (Jama community)](https://community.jamasoftware.com/discussion/26341/velocity-api-help)
</agent-message>
```

````
  **Cloud vs Data Center.** Commands decide from `ctx.kind`, never from the URL. `auth()` and the Cloud-only `email` field decide from the saved URL, as the plan says.
  
  | | Cloud | Data Center |
  |---|---|---|
  | Login | Basic, email and API token | Bearer, personal access token |
  | API version | v3 | v2 |
  | `whoami` user and link | `accountId`, link `jira/people/<accountId>` (the `:` is kept unescaped) | `name`, link `secure/ViewProfile.jspa?name=` |
  | Search | `/rest/api/3/search/jql`, paged by `nextPageToken`; `startAt` is never sent | `/rest/api/2/search`, paged by `startAt`/`maxResults`/`total` |
  | Description | ADF, flattened with `text.adf_to_text` | wiki text, kept as is |
  | Change history | separate `/issue/{key}/changelog` endpoint, paged until `isLast`, up to 1000 | `expand=changelog` on the same issue request, one call |
  
  - Descriptions are clipped to 4000 characters on both.
  - Change history is sorted oldest first on both, one entry per changed field.
  - Sprints use `/rest/agile/1.0/board/{id}/sprint` on both, paged until `isLast`.

  1. **Cloud login in tests.** Tests point at a fake server on 127.0.0.1, so forcing `kind="cloud"` still gets the Data Center login from `auth()`. This works, but the plan's "tests force kind" note does not cover the login.
  2. **End-to-end CLI tests are Data Center only.** `run_cli` works out the kind from the saved URL, which is always 127.0.0.1 here. Cloud is covered through `run_command`.
  3. **Sprint `board` field.** The plan gives `board` without saying where it comes from. I used the board id you pass in, not the sprint's `originBoardId`, so it matches the board in the `url`.
  4. **Needs a live server:**

     - That the Cloud people link `jira/people/<accountId>` opens with the raw colon.

     - That `secure/RapidBoard.jspa?...&sprint=` still opens the board on current Cloud, which may redirect to the newer boards URL.
````

````
  ```
  ---
  name: connectors
  description: Read facts from Jira, Confluence, Bitbucket Data Center, Jama and Jenkins through the kit's read-only connectors, and cite each item's link. Use when the person asks about issues, sprints, pages, pull requests, branches, requirements, test runs, builds or test reports in those tools, says "connect Jira" (or another tool), or a connector command fails or says it is not connected.
  ---
  
  # Connectors (read-only)
  
  The kit reads five tools from the command line: `jira`, `confluence`, `bitbucket` (Data Center), `jama` and `jenkins`. The person saves their own login once, in their own terminal. You only run the read commands below, from the repo root, and relay what they return.
  
  ## Rules
  
  - **Never touch a secret.** Never ask for, accept, look at, paste, store, repeat or write down a token, password, API key or client secret. Never set an `AI_SDLC_*` variable for the person, and never open or print the files in `~/.config/ai-sdlc/connectors/` (or `$AI_SDLC_CONFIG_DIR`). If the person pastes a secret into the chat, do not use it or repeat it. Tell them: "Please revoke that token now (it was shared in a chat) and create a new one; then save it yourself with the connect command in your own terminal."
  - **Read-only.** These commands only read. Never say you created, changed, moved, commented on, approved, posted or triggered anything in those tools. To change something there, write the text for the person to post themselves.
  - **Cite every item.** Every item in the output has a `url`. Put the link next to each fact you use. No link, no claim.
  - **Facts only.** Summarise what the output says; never invent an issue, field, status, date or person. If a field is `null` or missing, say it is not set. If `truncated` is `true`, say there are more and offer a higher `--limit`.
  - **Evidence, not verdicts.** Say "evidence found" or "evidence not found", never "compliant", "done" or "approved". The person decides.
  - **The team, not individuals.** Talk about the work, the flow and the team. Never rank, compare or judge people by their issues, pull requests, builds or test results.
  
  ## Is it connected?
  
  Run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connections`. It lists each connector with its URL, user, kind, last test and source (file or environment), never a secret.
  
  If the one you need is not connected (or a command exits with code 3), tell the person to run this **themselves, in their own terminal** (not through you). It asks for the URL and login, and types secrets hidden:
  
      python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect <name>
  
  When they say it is done, or ask you to test it, run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect <name> --test`. It uses the saved login, asks nothing, and makes one read-only call. `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py disconnect <name>` deletes a saved login, if they ask.
  
  ## Read data
  
      python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py <name> <command> [arguments] --json
  
  Always add `--json`. The output is `{"connector", "command", "source", "item"}` for one thing, or `{"connector", "command", "source", "items", "count", "truncated"}` for a list. Quote JQL, CQL and other text with spaces in double quotes. `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py <name> <command> -h` shows every option.
  
  | Connector | Commands |
  |---|---|
  | `jira` | `whoami` · `search "<JQL>" [--limit N] [--fields a,b]` · `issue <KEY> [--changelog] [--links]` · `sprints <board-id> [--state active,future,closed] [--limit N]` |
  | `confluence` | `whoami` · `page <id> [--format text\|storage] [--max-chars N]` · `search "<CQL or words>" [--space KEY] [--limit N]` |
  | `bitbucket` | `whoami` · `prs <project>/<repo> [--state OPEN\|MERGED\|DECLINED\|ALL] [--limit N]` · `pr <project>/<repo>/<id> [--diff] [--comments]` · `branches <project>/<repo> [--filter TEXT] [--limit N]` |
  | `jama` | `whoami` · `item <id>` · `search "<words>" [--project ID] [--type ID] [--limit N]` · `relationships <id> [--direction up\|down\|both]` · `testruns (--cycle ID \| --plan ID) [--limit N]` |
  | `jenkins` | `whoami` · `job <folder/job>` · `build <folder/job> <number\|last\|lastSuccessful\|lastFailed>` · `tests <folder/job> <number\|last> [--all]` |
  
  Exit codes: `0` ok, `1` error (a plain message on stderr), `2` wrong arguments, `3` not connected.
  
  ## When it fails
  
  Relay the message in plain words, then the fix:
  
  | The message says | Tell the person |
  |---|---|
  | not connected (exit 3) | Run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect <name>` in your own terminal. |
  | 401 Unauthorized | The login was not accepted (wrong or expired token, or the wrong user or email). Create a new token and run the connect command again, in your own terminal. |
  | 403 Forbidden | You are signed in, but this account may not read that. Ask the tool's admin for read access. |
  | 404 Not Found | Check the key, id or path, and that this account can see it. |
  | TLS certificate could not be verified | Your company probably uses its own certificate authority. Ask IT for its PEM file, then run the connect command again and give that file as the CA bundle. Verification is never switched off. |
  | proxy, 407, tunnel failed | The company proxy blocked or wants a login. Check `HTTPS_PROXY` / `NO_PROXY` with IT. |
  | name could not be found (DNS), timeout, connection refused | Check the URL, and whether the VPN is on. |
  | 429 or 5xx | The server is busy or had a problem. Try again in a minute. |
  
  Never work around an error by asking for the login, by calling the tool's web API another way, or by switching certificate checks off.
  
  ## Which connectors fit a role
  
  The person's setup summary names the connectors their roles usually need. Suggest those first; anyone may connect any of the five.
````

````
  ## Full text: "2. Update the kit to a newer version"
  ````
  ## 2. Update the kit to a newer version
  
  ### How to tell you need it
  
  - `check` (or the line Copilot shows at the start of a session) reports **`stale-kit`**, or **`kit-copy:<folder>`** with *"A newer kit (…) is waiting"*.
  - A command fails with **`invalid choice`**. For example, `setup.py connect jira` gives `invalid choice: 'connect' (choose from setup, change, update, check, ack, remove)`. Your kit is older than the command.
  - The README or this guide describes a feature your kit lacks. To see what you have, run `cat .ai-sdlc/kit/VERSION`, or check for a file such as `ls .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py`. Features on `main` can arrive before the version number changes, so a newer kit may show the **same** number.
  
  ### Step 1: get the newer kit
  
  - **You have a clone:** `cd ~/ai-sdlc-kit-source && git pull`
  - **You use ZIPs:** download it again, from **Code → Download ZIP** (the latest `main`) or from a release, and unzip it.
  
  ### Step 2: copy it into your repo, without `.git`
  
  Use any folder name inside the repo:
  
  ```bash
  cd ~/work/my-repo
  rsync -a --exclude .git ~/ai-sdlc-kit-source/ ./ai-sdlc-kit-new/
  ```
  
  ### Step 3: update
  
  **(a) Copilot:** say **"update the kit"**.
  
  **(b) Terminal:** run the `setup.py` **of the newer copy**, with `update`:
  
  ```bash
  python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update
  ```
  
  The whole recipe, for a clone:
  
  ```bash
  (cd ~/ai-sdlc-kit-source && git pull)
  rsync -a --exclude .git ~/ai-sdlc-kit-source/ ./ai-sdlc-kit-new/
  python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update
  ```
  
  ### What happens
  
  - `.ai-sdlc/kit` is replaced by the newer copy, and the copied folder (`ai-sdlc-kit-new/`) disappears: it was moved there.
  - Your choices stay: name, roles, language, git comfort, session summary, skills added or left out. Files you never edited are refreshed.
  - A file **you edited** is kept as it is. If the kit's version of it changed, the new one is put next to it as `<file>.kit-new`.
  - An **older** copy is refused and nothing changes: *"This copy is older (0.4.0) than the kit set up here (0.4.1). Nothing was changed."*
  - A copy **outside** the repo is refused: *"Copy the newer kit folder into the repo first; it is at …"*
  - `git status` stays clean.
  
  Real output, with one edited file:
  
  ```text
  $ python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update
  Updated to AI-SDLC 0.4.1 for Ana: Product Owner, QA · English.
  - Hidden from git: .ai-sdlc/ and every ai-sdlc-* file.
  - Wrote 1 file(s): .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md.kit-new
  - Kept your edit in .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md. The kit's copy, if it changed, is next to it as .github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md.kit-new.
  - Skills: … · git: hidden · session summary: on
  - Connectors for your roles: jira, confluence, jama, jenkins (say 'connect jira')
  Check: all good.
  $ git status --short
  $
  ```
  
  > **Same version number?** If the newer copy has the same number as your kit (common when you take the latest `main`), `check` calls it *"a copy of the kit that git does not hide. Delete it."* If you copied it in to update, ignore that and run `update` anyway. It works, and it adds whatever the newer copy has. This is how a 0.4.0 release kit gets `connect`.
  
  ### Compare a `.kit-new` file with your edited one, and choose
  
  See the difference:
  
  ```bash
  F=.github/instructions/ai-sdlc-po.instructions.md        # the file the summary named
  diff "$F" "$F.kit-new"                                   # or: code --diff "$F" "$F.kit-new"
  ```
  
  `code --diff` opens a side-by-side view in VS Code. Or say to Copilot: *"compare my ai-sdlc-po instructions with the .kit-new and explain the differences"*.
  
  Then choose:
  
  - **Take the kit's version** (recommended). Move any personal lines to your [personal notes](#7-personal-notes-and-skills) first, so you keep them. Then:
  
    ```bash
    mv "$F.kit-new" "$F"
    python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change          # no options: records the file; check is clean again
    ```
  
    Until you run `change`, `check` reports `missing:<file>.kit-new`. That is expected.
  - **Keep your version.** Leave both files as they are. The `.kit-new` file is hidden from git and harmless. If you delete it, `check` reports it as `missing:`, and the next `change` or `update` puts it back. To keep your own rules without this, put them in your personal notes and take the kit's version.
  ````
  
  ## Full text: "9. Troubleshooting"
  ```
  ## 9. Troubleshooting
  
  | Symptom | Cause | Fix |
  |---|---|---|
  | `invalid choice: 'connect' (choose from setup, change, update, check, ack, remove)` | Your kit is older than the command. | [Update the kit](#2-update-the-kit-to-a-newer-version): get the latest kit, copy it in without `.git`, run `python3 <copy>/setup.py update`. |
  | `setup.py: error: unrecognized arguments: jira` (after `setup.py setup jira`) | `setup` is the one-time setup, not the connect command. | `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect jira` |
  | `A kit is already set up in this repo (.ai-sdlc/kit)` | You ran `setup` with a newer copy. | Run `update` instead: `python3 <copy>/setup.py update`. |
  | `This copy is older (…) than the kit set up here (…)` | The copy you used is older than your kit. | Nothing changed. Delete that copy; get the latest one. |
  | `Copy the newer kit folder into the repo first` | The copy is outside the repo. | `rsync -a --exclude .git <kit>/ ./ai-sdlc-kit-new/`, then run `ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update`. |
  | `python3: command not found`, or `AI-SDLC needs Python 3.9 or newer` | Python is missing or older than 3.9 (`python3 --version`). | Ask IT to install Python 3.9 or newer on the VM. Nothing was changed. |
  | `git status` shows kit files | (1) A kit copy you put in is waiting for `setup` or `update`. (2) After `remove`: your edited files and personal notes were kept. (3) `check` reports `unexcluded:`. (4) Someone ran `git add -f`. | (1) Finish the setup or update, or delete the copy. (2) Delete or move them. (3) `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py change`. (4) `git restore --staged <file>`; never commit them. |
  | `The TLS certificate of <host> could not be verified` | Your company inspects TLS traffic with its own certificate authority. | Get the CA bundle (PEM) from IT, then run `connect <name>` again and give its path, or set `AI_SDLC_CA_BUNDLE`. See [Corporate network](#corporate-network-proxy-and-company-certificates). |
  | `The proxy … asks for credentials (407 Proxy Authentication Required)` | The proxy needs a login. | Ask IT how to set the proxy for command-line tools on the VM (often `HTTPS_PROXY=http://user:[REDACTED:url-credentials]@proxy:port`, or a proxy that does not need a login). |
  | `The proxy … refused the connection` / `could not be found`, or a `timeout` | Wrong proxy address, VPN off, or the tool's host must bypass the proxy. | Check `HTTPS_PROXY` and the VPN. For an internal host, add it to `NO_PROXY`. |
  | `The name <host> could not be found (DNS)` | Wrong URL, or you need the VPN. | Check the URL in `connections`; connect the VPN. |
  | `401 Unauthorized from <host>` | The token is wrong or expired, or the wrong user or email. For Jama: the client ID or secret. | Create a new token and run `connect <name>` again. Atlassian Cloud needs your **email** plus an API token; Data Center needs a personal access token. |
  | `403 Forbidden from <host> for <path>` | You are signed in, but this account may not read it. | Ask for read access. After several failed logins, some servers want one sign-in in the browser first. |
  | `404 Not Found from <host> for <path>` | The id, key or path is wrong, or your account cannot see it. Or the URL in `connect` has an extra path. | Check the id (Confluence page id, Jama API id, `PRJ/repo`, Jenkins job path). Check the saved URL with `connections`. |
  | `<Tool> is not connected` (exit code 3) | No saved login for that tool. | `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect <name>`, in your own terminal. |
  | `connect asks for secrets, so it runs only in your own terminal` | `connect` was run through Copilot or a script, without a terminal. | Open a terminal (**Terminal → New Terminal**) and run it there yourself. |
  | `Bitbucket Cloud is not supported yet` | The URL is `bitbucket.org`. | Only Bitbucket Data Center is supported. |
  | The document skills (Word, Excel, PowerPoint, PDF) cannot install their library: `ensurepip is not available`, or `python3 -m venv` fails | The `python3-venv` package is missing on the VM. | Ask IT to install `python3-venv`. The skills install into `~/.ai-sdlc/venv` only, and only with your consent. |
  | The document skills: `pip` cannot reach the package index | A company proxy or internal package mirror is needed. | Ask IT for the pip proxy or index settings (for example `HTTPS_PROXY`, or `pip config set global.index-url <mirror>`), then try again. |
  | Copilot says it cannot find `ONBOARDING.md`, or does something else when you say "do the onboarding" | Copilot did not look in the kit folder. Its file search may skip folders that git ignores, such as `.ai-sdlc/kit/` after setup. | Point it there: *"follow ai-sdlc-kit/ONBOARDING.md"* before setup (your folder name), or *"follow .ai-sdlc/kit/ONBOARDING.md"* after setup. |
  | `There is no skill <name>. Available: …` | A wrong skill name in `--add-skill`. | Use a name from the list it prints. |
  | `The kit is not set up in this repo yet` | `change`, `update`, `ack` or `remove` in a repo without the kit. | Check you are in the right repo (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`), or do the [first-time setup](#1-first-time-setup-in-a-repo). |
  
  Still stuck? Run `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py check` and `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connections`, and share the output with the kit owner. It never contains a secret. For connector problems, `AI_SDLC_DEBUG=1 python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py <name> whoami` also prints each request, with the login hidden.
  ```
````

````
  ## [0.5.0] — 2026-10-08
  ### Added
  - **Personal connectors, read-only:** `jira` and `confluence` (Data Center and Cloud), `bitbucket` (Data Center), `jama` (Jama Connect, OAuth client credentials) and `jenkins`. Copilot reads with `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/connectors.py <name> <command> [--json]`; every item carries its `url`. Only GET requests are sent (plus Jama's token request). Stdlib only; proxies and a company CA bundle are honoured, TLS verification is never switched off.
  - `setup.py connect <name> [--test]`, `connections` and `disconnect <name>`. The person runs `connect` in their own terminal (secrets typed hidden; it refuses without a terminal unless every value is in `AI_SDLC_<NAME>_*` variables). Logins are saved per user in `~/.config/ai-sdlc/connectors/` (0700/0600), outside every repo; `remove` never touches them, and no command prints a secret.
  - `connectors` skill in the core pack (`ai-sdlc-connectors`): how Copilot checks connections, reads data, cites links, stays read-only, explains errors, and never handles a secret.
  - Connector defaults per role in `roles/<id>/role.json` (suggestions only; anyone can connect any tool). The setup summary names them ("Connectors for your roles: …", marking connected ones).
  - `docs/how-to.md`: step-by-step guide (first-time setup, update, connect and use the connectors, change, check, personal notes and skills, remove, troubleshooting).
  
  ### Changed
  - `ONBOARDING.md` gains "Connect a tool", and its close names the connectors for the person's roles without asking for a login.
  
  ### Fixed
  - `check` no longer tells a person to delete a kit copy with the **same** version as the installed kit, which they may have copied in to update. It now says: "Another copy of the kit (same version X) is in <folder>. If you copied it in to update, say "update the kit"; otherwise delete it." Newer and older copies keep their messages; the finding id stays `kit-copy:<folder>`.

  ## What's new in 0.5.0
  
  ### Connect your own tools (read-only)
  
  Copilot can now read **Jira** and **Confluence** (Data Center and Cloud), **Bitbucket Data Center**, **Jama Connect** and **Jenkins** for you. The connectors only read: nothing is created, changed, commented on or posted in those tools.
  
  - **How to connect.** Say *"connect Jira"* (or Confluence, Bitbucket, Jama, Jenkins). Copilot tells you whether it is already connected and gives you the command to run **yourself, in your own terminal**: `python3 .ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py connect jira`. It asks for the URL and your login, hides what you type for secrets, and tests the connection once.
  - **Your credentials stay with you.** Logins are saved only on your computer, in your home folder (`~/.config/ai-sdlc/connectors/`, readable only by you), outside every repo. The AI never asks for, sees or stores a token or password, and `connect` refuses to ask for one through an assistant. `remove` never touches your logins.
  - **What Copilot can do with them.** Look up issues and sprints, search and read pages, review pull requests and branches, read requirements and test runs, check builds and test reports. Every item comes with its link, and Copilot says when it found nothing.
  - **Other commands.** `connect <name> --test` checks a saved login, `connections` lists what is connected (never a secret), `disconnect <name>` forgets a login.
  - **Role defaults.** The setup summary suggests the tools your roles usually need (for example Jira, Confluence and Jama for a Product Owner; Bitbucket, Jira and Jenkins for a Developer). These are only suggestions: anyone can connect any tool.
  
  ### A step-by-step guide
  
  [`docs/how-to.md`](https://github.com/danemil/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/blob/v0.5.0/docs/how-to.md) walks through first-time setup, updating the kit, connecting and using the connectors, changing preferences, checking your setup, personal notes and skills, removing the kit, and troubleshooting (proxies, company certificates, common errors).
  
  ### Fixed
  
  - `check` no longer tells you to delete a kit copy that has the **same** version as yours. It now says: *"Another copy of the kit (same version X) is in <folder>. If you copied it in to update, say "update the kit"; otherwise delete it."*
  
  ## Upgrading from 0.4.0
  
  1. **Get the newer kit:** `git pull` in your clone of the kit, or download this release's ZIP and unzip it.
  2. **Copy it into your repo, without `.git`**, under any folder name, for example:
     `rsync -a --exclude .git <kit folder>/ ./ai-sdlc-kit-new/`
  3. **Update:** say *"update the kit"* to Copilot, or run `python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update`.
  
  You should see `Updated to AI-SDLC 0.5.0 …`. Your choices stay, files you edited are kept (the kit's newer copy goes next to them as `<file>.kit-new`), and `git status` stays clean. Then say *"connect Jira"* (or another tool) whenever you are ready.
  
  ## Still to confirm on live servers
  
  The connectors are tested against each vendor's documented API shapes, but a few details could not be checked without a live server (for example some Jama field names and Jenkins aggregated test reports). If something looks off, check this list first: [connectors design §5.1](https://github.com/danemil/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/blob/v0.5.0/docs/roadmap/2026-10-08-connectors-design.md#51-needs-live-confirmation).
  
  Full details: [`CHANGELOG.md`](https://github.com/danemil/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/blob/v0.5.0/CHANGELOG.md).
  ```
````

````
  ## Summary
  
  The fix is committed on `fix/update-validates-copy` as **24eef83**, on top of origin/main 9bae0af. It is not pushed.
  
  - **Worktree:** `/Users/emidan/work/NOTES/20 Projects/FRQ/frq-ai-sdlc-boostrap-kit/.claude/worktrees/agent-a257a6bfab25f9895`
  - **Commit message:** exactly as specified, ending with the Co-Authored-By line.

  **One test fails, and I left it alone on purpose.** `test_release.test_the_newest_changelog_entry_is_the_version_and_unreleased_is_empty` requires `[Unreleased]` to be empty. The CHANGELOG line you asked for breaks that, so the suite fails on both Pythons until the 0.5.1 release step moves the line under a version. I did not loosen the test because that would mean deleting an assertion.
  
  ## Validation design
  
  `place.validate_kit(kit) -> list[str]` returns problems as short phrases, mostly `missing <kit path>`. It writes nothing. It checks:
  1. **Required files:** `setup.py`, `VERSION`, `ONBOARDING.md`, `connectors.py`, `roles/core/role.json`. It also checks that VERSION is a version number.
  2. **Every `roles/*/role.json`:** it must parse and have `skills` and `connectors` lists. Its `instructions.md` and `source` file must exist.
  3. **Connectors:** every connector a pack names must have `scripts/personal/connectors/<name>.py`.
  4. **Skills:** for every skill the packs name, plus every skill in the library, `SKILL.md` must exist. Every kit file it links to must exist too, whether inside or outside the skill folder.
  5. **Dry run:** only when 1–4 pass, it renders `wanted_files` for all roles plus all library skills. Any exception there becomes a problem.
  
  It does not catch a missing non-SKILL.md file inside a skill folder unless SKILL.md links to it. Without a manifest of kit files there is no way to know such a file should exist.
  
  Where it is used, in `commands.py`:
  - **`setup`:** `is_kit` stays as the quick "is this a kit at all" check. Then `_need_whole_kit` runs before `exclude.protect` and `move_kit`, so even `.git/info/exclude` is untouched on refusal.
  - **`update`:** the same check runs before `protect` and `replace_kit`. `update` also now builds every file from the copy *before* it moves anything. The only work left after the move is the file writes and the state save.
  - **Refusal messages:** they list up to three problems, then "…":
    - From a copy: "This kit copy is incomplete (…). Copy the whole kit folder again (without .git) and retry. Nothing was changed."
    - From `.ai-sdlc/kit` itself: "The kit folder .ai-sdlc/kit is incomplete (…). Copy the whole kit folder into the repo again (without .git) and run python3 <that folder>/setup.py update|setup. Nothing was changed."
  
  Other robustness changes:
  - **Unexpected errors:** `setup.py main()` catches them and prints a plain message with **exit code 4**. For `update` it says to run `.ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py update` to finish; for other commands, to run the same command again. `AI_SDLC_DEBUG=1` prints the traceback. The exit codes are now listed in the docstring: 0 done, 1 check findings, 2 refused, 3 not connected, 4 unexpected.
  - **Copy missing its scripts:** `setup.py` catches the `ImportError` and prints "This kit copy is incomplete …" with exit 2.
  - **`replace_kit`:** it now checks the copy is inside the repo *before* it deletes the old kit folder.
  - **Dropped skills:** if the newer kit no longer has one of the person's extra skills, `update` drops it and says so. Before, that crashed after the move.
  - **Not in the brief:** `setup` from a whole copy now replaces a kit folder left without `state.json`. That is what 0.5.0 leaves after a failed setup. Before, `setup` said "already set up" and `update` said "not set up", so the person was stuck.
  
  Docs:
  - `docs/how-to.md` has the "This kit copy is incomplete / kit folder … is incomplete" row, plus one for the exit-4 message.
  - `ONBOARDING.md`: two one-line additions. One is in Update step 3; the other is on the `stale-kit` advice: if it says incomplete, copy the whole kit in again and update from that copy. The parallel onboarding-connectors work may conflict here.
  - `CHANGELOG.md`: a line under Unreleased → Fixed.
  
  ## Before / after (real output, scratch dir `.../scratchpad/fix051/`)
  
  The full transcripts are `before.txt`, `after.txt` and `recover.txt`; the scripts are `demo.py`, `recover.py` and `demo_lib.py`.
  
  **Before** (0.5.0, `update` from a 9.9.9 copy missing `template/.claude/skills/connectors/SKILL.md`):
  ```
  FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '.../repo/.ai-sdlc/kit/template/.claude/skills/connectors/SKILL.md'
  [exit 1]
  # kit folder 9.9.9, state.json 0.5.0, copy still there: False
  - [stale-kit] The kit folder is 9.9.9 but this setup is 0.5.0. Say "update the kit".
  ```
  `.ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py update` then hit the same traceback. `setup` from an incomplete copy also tracebacked after moving the kit and writing the exclude block.
  
  **After:**
  ```
  $ python3 ai-sdlc-kit-new/setup.py update
  This kit copy is incomplete (missing template/.claude/skills/connectors/SKILL.md). Copy the whole kit folder again (without .git) and retry. Nothing was changed.
  [exit 2]
  # kit folder 0.5.0, state.json 0.5.0, copy still there: True
  ```
  `setup` gives the same message with exit 2: the copy is still there, there is no `.ai-sdlc`, and the exclude file is untouched.
  
  **Recovery from the real 0.5.0 leftover** (half-updated, with the incomplete copy installed):
  - Running `update` from a whole copy of the fixed kit gives "Updated to AI-SDLC 0.5.1", then "Check: all good.", and git status is clean.
  - The 0.5.0 failed-setup leftover is fixed the same way: `setup` from a whole fixed copy gives "Set up AI-SDLC …" and "Check: all good.".
  
  One limit: in that leftover state, `.ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py update` alone still runs the incomplete 0.5.0 code and tracebacks. So recovery there needs a whole new copy, which is what the new ONBOARDING line tells Copilot to ask for. With the fixed kit installed:
  - A half-update caused by an unexpected error is finished by `.ai-sdlc/kit/setup.py update` (there is a test for this).
  - An incomplete installed folder is refused plainly with exit 2.
  
  ## Tests
````
